## §5 — CROSS-CUTTING / CONTINUOUS (debt, forgotten-features, hygiene)

> Attended to in parallel/background. Each item closes when the workstream of its area touches it (Bucket B of the inventory) or in a dedicated sweep (Bucket C). The IDs `SMELL-*`/`FN-*`/`§N.N` keep their traceability to the backlog + normalization roadmap.

### 5.1 Architecture / quality (inventory §3)

- **TARGET-ARCH — target design + compliance (P1)** — design in [`docs/architecture/TARGET_ARCHITECTURE_CANON_ES.md`](https://github.com/edwardricardo/omni-post/blob/0086df48af32db3980680158e8b76ada41358dbf/docs/architecture/TARGET_ARCHITECTURE_CANON_ES.md) (it was deleted from the repository on 2026-10-17; git history keeps it and the link leads to its last version): an application core consumable by ALL the deployables, apps = deployment containers, boundaries **enforced** (not by convention), one composition root per executable. **Compliance TODAY = partial:** the core is still trapped in `apps/api` (→ `ARCH-WORKERS-PRISMA` below) and the boundary guard was partial (`SMELL-40`: the depcruise rule did not cover `@packages/api-common`). **FUTURE compliance:** complete the dependency-cruiser gates so that every violation of the core's boundary fails CI, and promote the core to `packages/@core` so that `apps/workers`/future ones consume it. **DoD:** (a) audit the current state vs the target map; (b) depcruise rules that enforce the core's boundaries in CI for every deployable; (c) the core promotion epic closed. It is the reference design of `ARCH-WORKERS-PRISMA` + `SMELL-45`.
- **ARCH-WORKERS-PRISMA** (SMELL-26/39, P1) — workers on direct Prisma, without use-cases/UoW/outbox. 🔗 blocks: Phase 2 recycling/moderation workers. Target design: `TARGET-ARCH` above.
- **ARCH-ROUTES-PRISMA** (WF2 `routes-prisma-03`, P1) — route handlers with a direct PrismaClient.
- **EVENT-DUAL-SYSTEM** (SMELL-10, P1) — outbox `EventDispatcher` vs a parallel `EventService`/Redis; the integration-events pipeline is dead in prod. Classify canon-vs-drift.
- **PROVIDER-CAPS-DRIFT** (SMELL-23/FN-042, P1) — `ProviderCapabilities` in 4 places + `ProviderId` `"twitter"` vs `"x"`. Unify.
- **WRK-MENTION-XTENANT** (assessment, HIGH — **it was not in this plan**, added 2026-08-16) — `MentionRepository.ts:62-67` deduplicates by `(provider, externalId)` **without `accountId`**, backed by a DB-level `@@unique([provider, externalId])` (`schema.prisma:2171`): the **second** tenant's mention collides with P2002 and is swallowed as an idempotent success → one tenant can poison/hide another's mention corpus. Verified live 2026-08-16. A candidate for N.A if it is confirmed exploitable without prior coordination.
- **tenant-health-idor-01** (assessment, MEDIUM) — `GET /health/tenant/:id/project/:id` **without authentication**, binding tenant context from a URL segment. It is purely the N.E axis; the plan only grazed it inside N-SEC-3. **The Product Owner's decision 2026-08-16:** it is deleted **only if** an equivalent per-account project health capability **already exists**; if it does not exist, it is **rebuilt properly** (authenticated, scope derived from the principal, an admin counterpart for parity) because knowing the health of the projects per account is part of multi-tenancy.
- **prov-x-media-auth-context-02** (`confirmed-adversarial`, HIGH) — the bearer-only `XApiClient` ignores OAuth1.0a → every post with media fails at upload. Align `REQUIRED_FIELDS`.
- **prov-classifier-403-drift-03** (`verified`, MEDIUM) — the 403→VALIDATION fix only in x/telegram/snapchat; pinterest/bluesky 403 non-auth pending. Extract a shared helper.
- **core-refresh-no-rotation-02** (`confirmed-adversarial`, HIGH) — customer token refresh without invalidation of the consumed one nor reuse detection; a leaked token survives logout 7d. Single-use blacklist + revoke-family.
- **AUTH-RL-1** `[M]` **API+ADM** — **Rate limits: the change `auth-rate-limit-integrity` enters the plan and the per-principal quota gets an owner.** `verified` 2026-10-05. The change exists only as a folder (`openspec/changes/auth-rate-limit-integrity/`, without `tasks.md`, last commit `d3479d08`) and no row of this plan names it; its C0 still requires `TRUSTED_PROXY_HOP_COUNT`, which ADR-0021 removed. Its design declares the per-principal quota "intention, with no owning change" (`design.md:368-402`), and the canon asks to prefer the authenticated principal over the IP (`SECURITY_CANON.md:96`). The live limiter groups by IP and the resource's route (`httpRateLimitPreHandler.ts:217,244`) and emits no metrics; the `api_rate_limit_*` counters (`apiMetrics.ts:271-311`) have no producer and label by `client_ip`. **DoD:** stage 1 — the design refreshed against ADR-0021 and executed (policy rows and an atomic C9: the 7 `config.rateLimit` blocks, `@fastify/rate-limit` and its test), together with the consolidation of `ADMIN_CUSTOMER_PARITY.md` §5 item 9 (a single source of rules); stage 2 — a second charge per account or user inside `requireClientAuth`/`requireAdminAuth`, with figures per role or plan that the Product Owner decides; each limiter decision as a metric with bounded labels (rule and result, never the IP) and a block-ratio alert with a runbook. It picks up the intention of `roleBasedRateLimit` (quotas per role, it closes L-26) and of `monitoring/rateLimitingDashboard.ts` (telemetry and abuse per tenant), 2026-03 code that never ran and leaves with cards C2 and C4 (§5.13). Order signed by the Product Owner on 2026-08-16: after `principal-derived-authority`. **Decision of PREG-8 (2026-10-06):** there is no in-application emergency mode of the limiter, which goes to the "Never" line (§4); the response to an attack goes at the edge, and `docs/runbooks/` gains the "edge under attack" runbook (the CDN's attack mode, the WAF's rate rules) when the deployment target exists, which is written then and not before. The row gains two points, and DEF-70 adds a third:
  - **Per-route switch:** turn off a non-critical surface or a limiter rule without deploying, with an audit of who and when; each group of routes carries a criticality label, so that load shedding can be added later (Stripe, "Scaling your API with rate limiters"; Google SRE, "Handling Overload").
  - **Shadow launch:** every new limiter rule first runs measuring what it would block, without blocking, and starts blocking when its metric justifies it.
  - **Input from DEF-70 (2026-10-09):** stage 2 picks up the intention of eleven deleted rules that never matched a route: publishing (`POST /sagas/post-publishing/start`, which also creates drafts), search (`GET /posts` with `searchText`, which shares a route with the listing), the analytics dashboard (`GET /analytics/dashboard`, polled every 30 s, and the SSE `GET /analytics/stream`, which asks for a cap of simultaneous connections per principal and not a per-minute bucket), the webhook history (`GET /webhooks/dashboard/events`), the Admin metrics (`GET /admin/dashboard/stats`, `GET /admin/analytics/metrics`) and the Admin audit (`GET /admin/audit/logs`, `GET /admin/compliance/audit-logs`, polled every 30 s); AI goes separately, to AI-QUOTA-1. Stage 1 replaces `contains`, which the table declares and never evaluates, with policy rows by pattern and method: today the row `/analytics/project/` with `contains: "/full"` also limits `GET /analytics/project/:projectId` to 5 per minute, and the row `/admin/accounts/` with `contains: "/usage"` leaves another 14 Admin routes at 10 per minute.
  - **Input from C-P1b (2026-10-16):** the customer's password-reset email has no per-address cap. `POST /auth/customer/request-password-reset` (`apps/api/src/auth/customerAuthRoutes.ts:632-641`) carries an inert `config.rateLimit` block (`:638`), the table `STANDARD_ROUTE_RULES` names only the customer's login and refresh (`httpRateLimitPreHandler.ts:92-93`) and the case falls into the default cap of 100 per minute per IP and route (`httpRateLimitPreHandler.ts:34`, `apps/api/src/index.ts:518`); `RequestPasswordResetUseCase` sends the email without consulting any per-address limit (`packages/core/customer-auth/src/RequestPasswordResetUseCase.ts:126-136`). Any unauthenticated caller can then have it sent to someone else's address, up to the route's per-IP cap, which is the same exposure the deleted-account notice had. C-P1b bounded that notice with `ThrottledDeletedAccountNoticeMailer` (`apps/api/src/infrastructure/adapters/`): one notice per address every 24 hours, through `CachePort`, with a SHA-256 key. Stage 1 of AUTH-RL-1 gives the reset email the same per-address cap. The Admin portal has the same gap with a stricter and live per-IP cap: `POST /admin/auth/password/reset` uses `rateLimit(3, 300000)` (`apps/api/src/admin/auth/adminAuthRoutes.ts:582`), with the key `admin:<ip>:<route>` (`adminAuthMiddleware.ts:228`), and `resetPasswordRequest` sends the email on every call for an active administrator (`adminAuthRoutes.ts:236-275`) with no per-address limit; the per-address cap covers both routes.
- **AI-QUOTA-1** `[M]` **API+PKG(core)+ADM** — **AI quota per account and spending limit at the providers** (added on 2026-10-09 by the closure of DEF-70). `verified` 2026-10-09. The AI routes have no limit of their own: the four rules that the HTTP limiter dedicated to them matched no route (DEF-70) and another six LLM routes never had a rule, so they run with STANDARD, per IP. The only outbound brake is a token bucket per provider and not per account (`apps/api/src/ai/orchestrator.ts:319` calls `tryConsume(providerName)`, with capacity `AI_PROVIDER_REQUESTS_PER_MIN`, 60 per minute, in `setupServices.ts:273-280`), so one tenant can exhaust it for everyone. Usage per account is recorded (`PrismaAiTokenUsageRepository.ts`, a monthly sum by `accountId`), and `SettingsService.ts:238-239` reads it together with the pool's `monthlyTokenBudget` budget; ADR-0030 sets 10,000 tokens per paid channel per month, 20,000 in the trial and 2,000 in the free plan. It remains to verify whether any AI path rejects the request when the account exhausts its allocation. The canon treats AI consumption unmeasured per tenant as a present exposure, not a future one: OWASP LLM10:2025 asks for "rate limiting and user quotas", OWASP API4:2023 asks for "spending limits for all service providers/API integrations" or, if they do not exist, billing alerts, and Anthropic and OpenAI limit by workspace or project. **DoD:** an AI request is rejected per account when its monthly ADR-0030 allocation is exhausted, with a test; the outbound brake divides the capacity by account, so that one tenant does not exhaust the others'; each configured provider has a spending limit or, if it does not offer one, a billing alert, documented in `docs/deployment/`. The per-minute rate of the AI routes per principal is set by stage 2 of AUTH-RL-1.
  - **Launch blocker** (the Product Owner, 2026-10-10): it closes within its turn, before the first real tenant uses the platform's AI.
  - **The Product Owner's model (2026-10-09):** each plan brings a monthly AI allocation (ADR-0030: per paid channel, BILL-21) and prepaid packs can be bought (BILL-31); a counter per account, with the consumption attributed to each workspace (the agency's client) and to each member, so that the owner sees who spends the most; the owner assigns quotas per workspace or per team, taken from the account's pool; the platform pays its providers by usage and reconciles that invoice with each tenant's consumption; if the account exhausts its allocation and has no pack, the whole plan continues except content creation with the platform's AI, and whatever runs with the tenant's own key (BYOK) continues as normal and is recorded without deducting.
  - **Market (2026-10-09):** among the social media tools there is "unlimited" AI with the plan (Buffer, Sendible, SocialBee), credits per plan (Later 5/50/100 per month with packs of 100; Ocoya; Vista Social, which cut theirs in 2026; Zoho Social, without rollover; Metricool, per brand; Sprout Social, per seat) and BYOK (Publer; Zoho Social as the way out when the credits run out; Sprinklr). None lets an agency's administrator assign quotas per client or per team: that pattern lives in AI and development platforms (Microsoft Copilot Studio divides a pool per environment; GitHub Copilot sets budgets per organization, cost center and user, and does not count BYOK usage; Anthropic limits per workspace and "Organization-wide limits always apply, even if workspace limits add up to more"; OpenAI limits per project). It is a differentiator for agencies.
  - **Measured state (2026-10-09):** `AiTokenUsage` (`infra/prisma/schema.prisma:3606-3617`) stores estimated tokens (characters / 4 or a fixed 1000, `apps/api/src/ai/orchestrator.ts:384,572`), because the adapters discard the provider's `usage`, and it does not record model, input and output, cost, workspace, member or function. Only what goes through `AiRequestService.executeRequest` is recorded: structured generation, embeddings, images, background jobs and every call without `accountId` use the platform's orchestrator without a record (`orchestrator.ts:171`, `apps/api/src/ai/aiService.ts:156-238,427-435`). There are three budgets that do not match: the one that is applied, providers × `accountCount` × 10,000 (`packages/core/ai/src/AiRequestService.ts:151-178`, failing open, with no reservation and no 429); the one the screen shows, a global `monthlyTokenBudget` of the platform (`packages/core/settings/src/SettingsService.ts:238-239`); and ADR-0030's, per paid channel. BYOK is stored per account (`AccountCredential`, group `AI_BYOK`) and recorded with `isByok`; in practice only the OpenAI key is used (`AiRequestService.ts:65-74`, `orchestrator.ts:191-208`). There is no "team" entity: the workspace is `Project` and the member has a role for the whole account and `ProjectMember.permissions` per workspace. There are no packs, no one-time purchases and no credit ledger.
  - **Open decisions for the Product Owner and the Maintainer when the turn comes**, with the recommendation in parentheses: (1) the unit: flat tokens, like ADR-0030, or a cost-weighted credit (a weighted credit, like GitHub and Metronome, because a token of an expensive model does not cost what a cheap one does; it amends ADR-0030 with a new ADR); (2) the semantics of the sub-quotas: a ceiling, like Anthropic, or a reservation, like Copilot Studio (a ceiling: the account always rules and never blocks unused balance); (3) the unit of allocation: workspace and member, which exist, or a new "team" entity (workspace and member first); (4) expiry and order of consumption (the plan's allocation expires at the end of the period without rollover and is consumed first; the packs afterwards, the one that expires sooner first, like Orb and Metronome); (5) on exhaustion: a hard cut of the platform's AI or overage billing (a hard cut with packs, the Product Owner's model; alerts at 75, 90 and 100 %); (6) the reconciliation: record per call the provider, the model, the input, output and cache tokens, the current price, the cost, the account, the workspace, the member and the function, and compare it daily with OpenAI's costs API and Anthropic's Usage & Cost Admin API, with provider projects or workspaces per environment and not per tenant (Anthropic admits 100 workspaces per organization); (7) the real-time control: a credit ledger of our own, append-only, that reserves before the call and settles with the real usage (Stripe's credits apply at invoicing and cannot cut usage at the moment).
  - **Expanded DoD:** the provider's real tokens with their cost; every platform AI path recorded and attributed; `accountId` always from the authenticated principal; a single budget, ADR-0030's or its replacement; BYOK exempt and visible separately as "not billed", with every provider the UI offers; exhaustion tests (the platform's AI is cut, the rest of the plan and BYOK continue); a daily reconciliation with a deviation alert. 🔗 dep: BILL-21 (the pool per channel), BILL-26 (the free plan's cap), N-SEC-4 (the AI cache without `accountId` records `tokensUsed=0` on hits), CRED-8b (a single source of the pool's keys), DEF-73 and DEF-74. The per-minute rate per principal of the AI routes is set by stage 2 of AUTH-RL-1; BYOK as a credential is already decided in §5.14 (D4).
- **SEC-KEK-1** `[S]` **API+DOCS** — **Key separation by purpose for the channels' OAuth tokens: decide it or declare it.** `pending-review`. `OAUTH_ENCRYPTION_KEY` (`config/env.ts:114`) was required at startup for "ProviderConnection token columns" (`secretCatalog.ts:76-79`), a model that does not exist; the OAuth tokens live in `Channel.credentials` under `PLATFORM_ENCRYPTION_KEY`, together with the rest of Class A (`secretCatalog.ts:71-74`), with AAD per channel and rotation by versions (`env.ts:113-129`). The repository already separates keys when the threat model changes (`env.ts:144-147`). The 2026-03 variable is deleted with card C3 without ever having had a reader. **DoD:** an ADR decides whether the tenants' OAuth tokens carry a KEK of their own, with its re-encryption path, or whether a single KEK covers Class A; `docs/security/SECRETS.md` aligned. 🔗 dep: DEF-29 (finding F1: AAD of `Channel.credentials` between API, seed and workers), closed first. **Related (2026-10-06):** ADR-0034 sets the key custody of the five encrypted families —a single key-encryption provider with one data key per record (decisions 4 and 5)— and retires `OAUTH_ENCRYPTION_KEY` (decision 13); this row is adjudicated against it.
- **SEC-SSRF-1** `[M]` **[SECURITY]** **API+CLI** — **SSRF control on the HTTP egress toward URLs that come from the tenant.** `pending-review`. There is no network guard for those egresses (`rg 'isPrivateIp|ssrf|isLoopback'` = 0); the live control is the media host list `makeMediaUrlSchema(ALLOWED_MEDIA_HOSTS)` (`security/inputValidation.ts:174,287`), and the portal's image optimizer accepts any https host (`apps/client/next.config.mjs:30-33`, `hostname: "**"`). The URL validation "with domain restrictions" of `validation/secureSchemas.ts:113-136` (2026-03, never connected; an `includes("localhost")`) is deleted with card C3. **DoD:** an inventory of the egress points toward tenant URLs, including the Next optimizer; an outbound client that resolves DNS and rejects loopback, private ranges, link-local and cloud metadata, also after redirects; negative tests per point.
- **AUTH-AUDIT-1** `[M]` **PKG(core)+API** — **Audit trail of the customer's authentication flows** (`ADMIN_CUSTOMER_PARITY.md` E1, §5 item 6). `verified` 2026-10-05. On the customer side only failures are recorded (`LOGIN_FAILURE` from `RedisBruteForceAdapter.ts:214-216`); the use cases of `packages/core/customer-auth` do not write `AuditLog` for a successful login, refresh, logout, registration or reset (`rg -i audit` only finds a comment). The forensic table `AdminLoginAttempt` (2026-03, no writes) is deleted with card C7; the admin side's trail lives in `AuditLog`. **DoD:** the use cases emit rows through an injected port, with a CUSTOMER actor, IP and agent; an integration test per flow. 🔗 related: N-AUTH-2 (the reader).
- **AUTH-OBS-1** `[M]` **API+INFRA** — **Observability and security alerts of authentication** (`ADMIN_CUSTOMER_PARITY.md` E9-E11, §5 item 15). `verified` 2026-10-05. Today there are two security alerts (`prometheus/alerts/api.yml:35-61`: MFA backup-code reuse and Admin refresh replay, over `api_security_threats_total`); there are no metrics of login attempts, MFA verifications, revocations or refresh failures, nor alerts of a brute-force spike, a burst of MFA failures or a mass revocation. **DoD:** `login_attempts_total{side,result}`, `mfa_verifications_total`, `session_revocations_total` and `token_refresh_failures_total` with bounded labels; alerts of a brute-force spike, a burst of MFA failures, a mass revocation and a mass modification or deletion by the same actor, each one with a runbook, the compromised-account one included. It picks up the intention of `security/auditLogger.ts` (threshold alerts over the audit trail) and of `BusinessKPITracker.trackUserEngagement` (active sessions), 2026-03 code that never ran and leaves with cards C4 and C6 (§5.13).
- **AUTH-RISK-1** `[M]` **API+PKG(core)+CLI+ADM** — **Risk-based login with the category's practice, without a scoring engine** (added 2026-10-06, decision of PREG-2). `pending-review`. Today there are counters per identifier and per IP with backoff, lockout, a CAPTCHA signal after 3 failures and `LOGIN_FAILURE` rows (`RedisBruteForceAdapter.ts:79-90,214-216`, `LoginCustomerUseCase.ts:55-57`), with no device memory and no sign-in notice. **DoD:** in the customer and Admin logins, a signed known-device cookie (a security cookie, exempt from consent according to WP29, Opinion 04/2012, §3.3); on an unknown device, an email verification before issuing the session when the user has no MFA, or the MFA request when they have it; an email on each new sign-in (device, approximate location, time) with a "it wasn't me" path, and on the addition or removal of an authenticator and on recovery (NIST SP 800-63B-4 §4.6); the account owner can require MFA of all the members, with a grace period; audit rows for everything. No risk engine is built (threat score, scored geolocation, device fingerprint); revisit if an enterprise customer requires it. The current counters, lockout, CAPTCHA and audit remain as the base layer. It picks up the intention of `deviceId` and `location` of `AdminLoginAttempt` (2026-03, no writes), which leaves with card C7 (§5.13). 🔗 dep: the customer's self-service MFA (§5.3.b), the decision on the customer's session model (question 2 of `ADMIN_CUSTOMER_PARITY.md` §7), AUTH-AUDIT-1, AUTH-OBS-1.
- **SUPPORT-ACCESS-1** `[M]` **API+ADM+PKG(core)** — **Support access to a tenant's data only with a time-bound grant** (added 2026-10-06, decisions of PREG-3 and PREG-10). `pending-review`. Today an operator reads across tenants through `withSystemContext` without leaving a trace at run time (question 6 of `ADMIN_CUSTOMER_PARITY.md` §7). **DoD:** an operator reaches the data of ONE tenant only with an active grant: a written justification, the ticket reference, a maximum duration in hours and step-up MFA on activating it; each read is audited with the grant's id, and the grant expires on its own; `withSystemContext` remains the code path and the grant becomes its precondition in support reads. It is the staff's only access to the customers' content: there is no cross-tenant content library (PREG-10). It answers question 6 of `ADMIN_CUSTOMER_PARITY.md` §7. 🔗 dep: N-SEC-5.b.
- **BREAKGLASS-1** `[S]` **ADM+INFRA+DOCS** — **Admin emergency accounts, sealed and watched** (added 2026-10-06, decision of PREG-3). `pending-review`. **DoD:** one or two emergency administration accounts outside SSO, with sealed credentials; each use alerts the owner and is audited; a runbook to use them and to review them after each use, with a test that they work at least every 90 days, as Microsoft recommends for its emergency access accounts. 🔗 dep: AUTH-OBS-1 (the alert).
- **ADM-JIT-1** `[M]` **API+ADM** — **Time-bound elevation of an administrator above their role** (added 2026-10-06, decision of PREG-3; a row recorded for later). `pending-review`. Roles stay for routine work; this row is scheduled when the number of operators grows. **DoD:** eligible versus active assignments, activation with a justification, optional approval, MFA on activation, a maximum duration, a notice on activation, periodic review and audit (Microsoft Entra PIM, Google Privileged Access Manager; NIST SP 800-53 AC-2(2) and AC-6), on the grant machinery of SUPPORT-ACCESS-1. It picks up the expiry intention of `AdminUserPermission` (2026-03, unused), which leaves with card C7 without reusing anything, and gap D14 of `ADMIN_CUSTOMER_PARITY.md`. 🔗 dep: SUPPORT-ACCESS-1.
- **SMELL-37** AdminAuthService Control Freak (audit off-spine + inline collaborators; the **MfaService** collaborator was extracted after N-SEC-5's port + DI, #129-133 — `PasswordService`/`SessionManager` remain) · **SMELL-47** 14 sites bypassing `AuditLogRepository` (4 of them —`security/credentialManager.ts` 1 and `security/auditLogger.ts` 3— are dead code that leaves with card C4 of §5.13: 10 remain) · **SMELL-44** ChannelAuthFailureRecorder overlap · **SMELL-28** `Account.subscription` dropped with 2 live refs (runtime bug, P1) · **SMELL-49** 48 packages/core noEmit → TS project references (an ADR of its own) · **SMELL-48** admin/client bundles over the limit · **SMELL-50** hoisting audit · casts/type-debt (SMELL-2/5/7/8/33).

### 5.2 Normalization roadmap (inventory §3A, canon)

§6.1 Containerization (P1, **UNPAUSED 2026-09-25** — see the [IMAGE] card below) · §3.1.b OpenAPI Zod ~342 routes (P1, it blocks the typed UI) · §3.2.b provider contract tests MSW (P1) · §4.1.b saga+outbox chaos (P1) · §4.2.b observability ops + alerts (P1) · §4.2.c alertmanager + routing of notifications to Slack/email/PagerDuty (P1; it picks up the channels that `performanceMonitor.ts` and `rateLimitingDashboard.ts` only simulated) · OBS-1 to OBS-4 (below) · §2.2.b coverage+mutation gates (P1) · §4.3 GDPR/retention (P2) · §6.2 Kubernetes (P2, 🔗§6.1; it includes deciding per-PR preview environments and their cleanup: the job `cleanup-preview-environments` of `cleanup.yml:496-560` pointed at an EKS cluster that never existed) · §5.2 queue triad (DEFERRED).

> **ℹ️ CARD [IMAGE]** — the four container images went from red since 2026-07-31 to verified, with six defects fixed, five of them invisible without running the image; landed with #304 (merge `9e5175e4`) on 2026-09-25, and re-pinned in `96df910` after CVE-2026-84782 (`docs/deployment/base-images.md`). The Maintainer's trigger decision of 2026-10-05 (option A: `production-ci.yml` on pull requests against `main`, `omni-post-cc`, `feature/**` and `workstream/**`, delivered by #419, merge `7d73b00d`) lives in [base-images.md](../deployment/base-images.md#when-ci-runs-the-images), with its cost, its reason and the options it rejected. Still named and open: 133M of build tooling in the production closure (`docs/deployment/image-build-model.md`) and three gaps of the import assertion in the build stage (a non-resolver error passes as success, there is no `timeout`, and the portal smoke assumes a single `EXPOSE`). The full card lives at [this file at `c8c62cbf`](https://github.com/edwardricardo/omni-post/blob/c8c62cbf06c1a38031a0c5a22b7339f120f50497/docs/product/MASTER_PLAN_ES.md).

**§4.2.b — alerts and dashboards over fed sources** (added 2026-10-05; the Maintainer's decision on card C6, amended by findings F5 and F6 of the §5.13 mapping): §4.2.b is built on series that some source feeds —ApiMetrics once the metrics plugin runs for every route (DC-1), the workers' metrics and those of the live breaker of `external-apis`— and never on instruments without samples. Today three of the four provisioned rules and the SLO availability query read series that nobody exports (DEF-34), and the business dashboards read recorders with no producer (DEF-35); the rows below make the live series real.

- [ ] **OBS-1** `[S]` **API+INFRA** — **Saturation of the API process and dependency health as fed series, with alerts.** `verified` 2026-10-05. The API does not register the process's default metrics (only the workers do: `apps/workers/src/publishWorker.ts:149`) and its gauges of memory, active connections, health and queue depth have no producer (`apiMetrics.ts:338-356`, setters without callers); the dashboards of `grafana/dashboards/system-resources.json` and the "Availability" alert that §4.2.b plans depend on them. **DoD:** the process's default metrics in the API; the health of the registered checkers as a series; heap/RSS, event-loop lag and availability alerts (with the real job label, see DEF-34), each one with a runbook. It picks up the intention of `monitoring/performanceMonitor.ts` (an alert for memory > 85 % and aggregated health; it ran without a reader) and of `health/healthMetrics.ts` (never registered), both from 2026-03, which leave with cards C1 and C6 (§5.13).
- [ ] **OBS-2** `[M]` **API+ADM** — **An "application behavior" view in Admin.** `pending-review`. The Maintainer's decision of 2026-10-05 (card C1): Admin reads Prometheus through the API, with PromQL over its HTTP API, and Prometheus is never exposed to the tenants. Today the operator only has Grafana (`grafana/dashboards/*.json`). **DoD:** permissioned Admin routes that return latency, errors and traffic per route, saturation, active alerts and limiter decisions (AUTH-RL-1); a page in Admin; no response mixes tenants' data. It picks up `PerformanceMonitor.getDashboardData` (it ran without a reader) and the `/admin/rate-limiting/*` routes of `rateLimitingDashboard.ts:123-215` (never registered), 2026-03 code that leaves with cards C1 and C4 (§5.13). 🔗 dep: DEF-34, DEF-35.
- [ ] **OBS-3** `[M]` **API+WRK** — **Technical observability per tenant.** `pending-review`. No HTTP series and no span carries the account (`rg 'setAttribute\("(tenant|account)'` = 0 in API and workers); only `tenantHealth.ts` emits gauges with `tenant_id` (`packages/monitoring/health-checks/src/tenantHealth.ts:22-40`). **DoD:** an `account_id` label only on chosen series of bounded cardinality; an `account.id` attribute on the API and workers spans; a per-account query in OBS-2. It complements `tenant-health-idor-01` (§5.1) and STOR-13. It picks up the intention of `PerformanceMonitor` according to the Product Owner (behavior per customer) and of `correlationTracking.ts` (`tenantId` in the spans), 2026-03 code that leaves with cards C1 and C4 (§5.13).
- [ ] **OBS-4** `[S]` **INFRA** — **Alerts and dashboards over the providers' live series.** `verified` 2026-10-05. The breaker of `packages/adapters/external-apis/src/circuitBreaker.ts:244-341` exports requests, failures, timeouts, state changes (`from_state`/`to_state`) and duration, and the workers measure the duration per provider (`apps/workers/src/metrics/workerMetrics.ts:203`); no rule and no dashboard queries them. **DoD:** alerts of an open breaker, error rate and latency per provider, and consumption of the provider's quota where its API reports it; a dashboard in Grafana; a runbook. The breaker's state is an alert signal, not a readiness one. It picks up the intention of `@monitoring/circuit-breaker` (four rules: open, error rate, slowness, recovered) and of `CircuitBreakerHealthChecker`, 2026-03 code that never received a single sample and leaves with card C6 (§5.13).

- [ ] **GUARANTEES-NEED-BACKING** `[M]` **DOCS+CI/P1** (new 2026-08-16, the Maintainer's decision) — **Every documented guarantee needs executable backing** (the rule lives in §0.2 point 4.c; here is the work of putting it in place). Two parts: (a) an **ADR** that sets the rule as canon — ⚠️ **the number is disputed**: `docs/technical/` has **two** `ADR-0020` (`audit-actor-exclusive-arc` 07-10 and `tenant-context-at-boundaries` 07-17), both `Accepted` and referenced; the first free number is **0021**, but if the collision is resolved by renumbering the later one, the new ADR is **0022**. Decide before writing it (cross-ref N-DOC-2). (b) An **audit pass** over the existing statements: each normative sentence of the 4 canon children + the JSDoc of `packages/ports/**` **+ `FEATURE_TRACE_MATRIX_ES.md` and `MULTI_TENANT_GUARDS.md`** (two of the seven false statements live there and would stay outside a scope limited to canon+ports) either names its test/fitness, or is rewritten as an intention. **DoD:** ADR accepted; the 6 false statements already identified corrected (§0.2 4.c lists them); a fitness or script that detects normative statements without declared backing, with a **measured baseline** (if a clean hard-zero is not possible, the approximation and its false-positive profile are documented, instead of a rule that nobody can enforce).
- **GUARD-NESTED-INCLUDES** `[M]` **INFRA/P0-security** (**= `SMELL-59` structural-hardening candidate, 2026-07-15 — promoted from a diff-gate suggestion to a change of its own**) — **The Prisma guard does not cover nested relation reads.** `tenantGuardCheck` receives **one** `model` and mutates only `args.where` (`infra/prisma/src/extensions/tenantGuard.ts:186-219`); Prisma's extensions intercept **top-level** operations, so an `include`/`select` travels uninspected **even if that model is enrolled** — `project` is, and it leaked anyway through the `include` of `GET /accounts` (N-AUTH-1, which deleted that route on 2026-10-18; the doctrine gap remains). **`MULTI_TENANT_GUARDS.md` never mentions nested `include`s and `tenantGuard.test.ts` does not have a single case.** It is a **doctrine** gap, not a route's: it affects every `include` of an enrolled model in the repo. A high-priority change of its own (the Product Owner's decision): a sweep of every `include`/`select`, a test case in `tenantGuard.test.ts`, and the correction of the canon. Its natural input is N-SEC-3.b's total registry (which tenant predicate applies to each target relation). 🔗 dep: N-SEC-3.b.
- **CI-CLIENT-DEPCRUISE** `[S]` **CI/P2** (P-VERIFY-1, 2026-06-29) — the CI depcruise does NOT scan `apps/client`, and `apps/client/tsconfig.json` has no `baseUrl` → madge/tsconfig-paths skips ~25% of the modules when resolving `@/*` (a blind scan). **DoD:** add `apps/client` to the CI depcruise scope with the `@/*` alias resolved (or `baseUrl` in the tsconfig), to gate the client's cycles/orphans (today 0 cycles verified, but without a gate).

**GO-LIVE gates (production):**

- [ ] **SECRETS-ROTATION-GOLIVE** `[M]` **[SECURITY, P0-at-go-live]** — when going to production: (a) rotate **all** the real secrets (DB, providers'/LLM API keys, JWT, etc.); (b) purge `.env` from git history (`git filter-repo`/BFG) until `git log --all -- .env` = 0 (today = 3 commits, incl. Genesis). **Context (the Maintainer's decision):** the current secrets are **dev-only**, created for development; the `.env` was tracked in Genesis (finding L-591) and was untracked (`78b1055c`) but it is still in the history — **an accepted risk** while they are dev-only; the definitive rotation + purge are done when moving to production. Guide: [`docs/security/T0A_SECRETS_ROTATION_RUNBOOK.md`](../security/T0A_SECRETS_ROTATION_RUNBOOK.md) (KEPT). **DoD:** secrets rotated in all the external services + `git log --all -- .env` = 0 + apps redeployed green.

The queues' raw statistics, job deletion and other accounts' jobs remain Admin's. 🔗 dep:N-COR-8.
- **SETTINGS-HUB** `[S]` **CLI** (added 2026-10-05, intention of C12) — the user menu links `/dashboard/settings` (`apps/client/app/[locale]/dashboard/layout.tsx:170`), which has no `page.tsx`; of the 11 settings sections, 6 have no link anywhere (`crm`, `integrations`, `referral`, `sso`, `team`, `usage`; `ADMIN_CUSTOMER_PARITY.md` F20). **DoD:** an index page that links the current sections; the menu link stops giving 404.
- **ADM-NOTIF-1** `[M]` **API+ADM** (added 2026-10-06, decision of PREG-10) — **Admin notification center.** Today Admin only has toasts (`apps/admin/lib/stores/notificationStore.ts` re-exports the toasts store and leaves with card C12) and every notifications route requires `requireClientAuth` (`apps/api/src/notifications/notificationRoutes.ts:407-477`); `ADMIN_CUSTOMER_PARITY.md` F7 counts it as the largest customer→admin gap. **DoD:** the Admin twin of the notifications module (SSE, bell, read and attended state, preferences per operator), fed by operator events —backlogged queues, publishing failures above a threshold, authentication failures with the providers, billing and gateway events, the tenants' health— from the live series and the outbox, never from the instruments without data that card C6 deletes; it closes question 4 of `ADMIN_CUSTOMER_PARITY.md` §7. It does not include a cross-tenant content library: staff see a tenant's content only with the grant of SUPPORT-ACCESS-1 (§5.1). 🔗 related: OBS-4, AUTH-OBS-1.

### 5.3.b Security self-service surface (customer) — backend live, UI absent

> **New subsection (2026-08-16).** §5.3 has the right shape ("backend ready, UI/wire absent") and **none** of these in its inventory. Source: `docs/architecture/ADMIN_CUSTOMER_PARITY.md`. It scales as **FE-only** work — but **after** N-SEC-6 and N-AUTH-2 repair the backends underneath, not before.

- **MFA self-service (customer)** — 5 live endpoints, **zero** UI (`rg 'auth/mfa' apps/client` = 0 hits). Admin does have its own.
- **Forgot / reset password** — the page **does not exist in any portal**; `apps/client/.../login/page.tsx:118` links `/forgot-password` and that route dir is not there. In admin it is worse than recorded: the **confirm** page exists but **not the request one**, and `POST /admin/auth/password/reset` has **zero** consumers → an admin cannot trigger the email whose token their own page consumes. 🔗 dep: N-SEC-6.
- **Change password while authenticated (customer)** — **the endpoint does not exist**; the portal calls `POST /auth/customer/change-password` and receives 404. Admin has the complete stack. 🔗 dep: N-SEC-6.
- **Activity / audit viewer** — a backend on both sides, a consumer on neither; the admin hooks (`useAuditLogs`/`useAuditStats`) are imported only by each other and their docblock points to a `/logs` page that is not in the tree, while `SidebarNav.tsx:88` publishes the link. 🔗 dep: N-AUTH-2.
- **API-key CRUD · analytics export · session management · profile editing** — the same pattern, a total or partial backend with no surface.
- **Restore a deleted account before login (customer)** — **does not exist** (neither backend nor UI). `POST /accounts/:accountId/restore` (`apps/api/src/accounts/accountRoutes.ts:781`) accepts the owner only with a client token that has `account:delete`, and `LoginCustomerUseCase` does not let the user of a deleted account in (`packages/core/customer-auth/src/LoginCustomerUseCase.ts:186-208`): the owner cannot get that token, so today only the admin with `account:manage` restores. A pre-login path is missing, for example a restoration link verified by e-mail. **The Product Owner's decision (2026-10-16, unit C-P1b):** a registration with the e-mail of a deleted account responds with the same `409 EMAIL_EXISTS` as a live account and sends an e-mail notice to the owner of the address, which today refers them to support; when this flow exists, the notice adds its link.
- **Definitive deletion by the customer themselves (customer)** — **does not exist** (neither backend nor UI). `DELETE /accounts/:accountId/hard` (`apps/api/src/accounts/accountRoutes.ts:791`) is admin-only: `requireAdminAuth`, `account:manage` and a written reason. What is missing is for the owner to complete the deletion of their deleted account, with the warning that it deletes all the account's data and cannot be undone. **The Product Owner's decision (2026-10-16, unit C-P1b):** the same e-mail notice as the previous item; when this flow exists, the notice adds its link.

> **Path correction for `admin-logs-gitignored-01` (§5.3) — the item is CORRECT, only its path went stale.** The page exists and has 410 lines: `apps/admin/app/[locale]/(dashboard)/logs/page.tsx`. The migration to `[locale]` (`e9ec43e1`) moved it to a directory that the unanchored rule `.gitignore:51` (`logs/`) now swallows, and the moved copy was never re-added (`git check-ignore -v` confirms it). The assessment's remedy (`git add -f` + **anchor the rule** to `/logs/` so that it does not capture app routes) is still the right one — it only has to be pointed there. ⚠️ **A tool gotcha that almost slipped a false reclassification in here:** `fd -H` shows hidden files but **still respects `.gitignore`** — to see an ignored file you need `fd -H -I`. A "does not exist" derived from `fd -H` over an ignored path is a false negative.

- **CQRSIntegration dead-route** `[S]` (P-VERIFY-2 bonus) — **RESOLVED:** `apps/api/src/cqrs/CQRSIntegration.ts` was confirmed DEAD_CODE (never wired in any composition root) and was removed from the repo (branch `workstream/cqrs-integration-purge`; merged to `main` via **PR #128**, 2026-07-19, +30/−2358); the pending classification is closed. In the same PR the `statements` coverage floor was re-baselined 55→54 (authorized). FN-009 (optimizedPostsRoutes) is still open: it is part of card C11 of §5.13, which waits for the approval of the Product Owner and the Maintainer. FN-010/027 (rateLimitingDashboard) **RESOLVED 2026-10-05 by card C4** (the Maintainer's decision): the file leaves with unit DC-4 of §5.13; the limiter's telemetry and alerts live in AUTH-RL-1 (§5.1), its Admin screen in OBS-2 and the routing of notifications in §4.2.c (§5.2). ⚠️ **Naming note:** the campaign's fact sheet labeled #128 as "N-SEC-3", but in this plan and in the repo's commits `N-SEC-3` is the guard for `projectId`-only models (§1.A, **IN PROGRESS**), NOT the CQRSIntegration purge. They are distinct items; do not confuse them.

### 5.9 Payments and media storage — decisions of the Product Owner and the Maintainer (2026-10-04)

> **ℹ️ CARD [PAYMENTS-STORAGE] — decisions on payment gateways and media storage, written down (2026-10-04).**
> **Status: decided and documented; nothing implemented. Queued: it does not start until N-TEST-1 (Testing Refoundation) and the legal register (§5.10), which goes first, have closed; its exact place is fixed at that moment (the Product Owner, 2026-10-05: "we already have a marked route, let's not deviate").** On 2026-10-04 the Product Owner and the Maintainer closed, after five research reports, the decisions on the payment gateways (Stripe and Paddle) and on media storage. They are recorded in six ADRs and two specifications so that they do not have to be asked again. That same night, after a code map and a sixth report, the Product Owner also closed the pricing model and the domain vocabulary (ADR-0030 and ADR-0031), which are integrated into the payments specification. The slices below are the pending work; each one is a PR of about 400 lines as a planning heuristic, with its tests and its documentation, and its DoD is that of its row in the specification plus the §0.2 bar.
>
> **Decisions:** [ADR-0024](../technical/ADR-0024-billing-currency-and-price-catalog.md) to [ADR-0031](../technical/ADR-0031-domain-vocabulary.md) hold each decision, the points resolved on 2026-10-04 and 2026-10-05, and the initial values, with their research sources; the open points are the "Open points" section of [billing-gateways.md](../features/billing-gateways.md) and, for the unit of the AI pool, decision (1) of AI-QUOTA-1 (§5.1); the specifications are [billing-gateways.md](../features/billing-gateways.md) and [media-storage.md](../features/media-storage.md). The findings F13 = SMELL-184 (the measured mechanism of N-COR-4, §1, N.B), F14 = SMELL-185, F15 = SMELL-186, F16 = SMELL-187 and SMELL-188 to SMELL-193 are in [roadmap-detected-smells-backlog.md](../reports/roadmap-detected-smells-backlog.md).

It changed on 2026-10-04: the catalog and its screens moved to BILL-18 and BILL-19. ADR-0024/0030. 🔗 dep:BILL-4, BILL-5, BILL-18.

ADR-0029.
- [ ] **STOR-9** **PKG+API** — `UploadTicket`, `verifyUpload` and a sweep in S3 and Spaces. ADR-0029. 🔗 dep:STOR-3.
- [ ] **STOR-10** **PKG** — `UploadTicket` and `verifyUpload` in GCS and Azure. ADR-0029. 🔗 dep:STOR-4, STOR-9.
- [ ] **STOR-11** **API+ADM+CLI** — global caps in Admin; the effective cap, format and duration before uploading and when publishing. ADR-0029. 🔗 dep:STOR-8, STOR-9.

- [ ] **STOR-14** **PKG+API+WRK+INFRA** — antimalware scanning by default (added on 2026-10-06, decision of PREG-7, §5.13; with no row in `media-storage.md` §10 yet): a neutral `MalwareScannerPort` that `verifyUpload` consumes; documents are scanned before the media leaves quarantine, and images are re-encoded without metadata with `sharp` (which also removes the EXIF location); the real adapter is ClamAV (`clamd` in `docker-compose.yml` beside MinIO and Vault, stream scanning; a `services:` entry in CI's integration job), with its real contract suite. A synchronous verdict model (ClamAV) and an asynchronous one (cloud scanners that tag the object after the upload): the media carries `scan: pending | clean | infected | error`, publishing waits for `clean` and an infected object is deleted or quarantined with an audit row and a message for the user; the switch `MALWARE_SCANNER` is validated at startup (`clamav` by default; `none` is rejected outside tests). Never VirusTotal's public API: its terms forbid commercial use. The unit reviews whether ADR-0029, which covers type, size, format and duration and names no antimalware control, needs an amendment; if a cloud scanner is activated, LEGAL-1 records it as a processor. 🔗 dep:STOR-9.
- [ ] **STOR-15** **PKG+CI** — cloud scanning adapters (added on 2026-10-06, PREG-7), written now from the official documentation and marked **UNCERTIFIED against the live service** in their header and in this row until their first live run in green: AWS GuardDuty Malware Protection for S3 (asynchronous: object tags and EventBridge toward the verdict job) and Microsoft Defender for Storage (asynchronous: blob tags and Event Grid); Google Cloud has no native object scanner, so its path is the ClamAV adapter, as in Google's reference architecture. A single contract suite: real against ClamAV, with the SDK mocked for the cloud adapters and a live batch under its own tier, which fails without credentials and is declared in `docs/development/TESTING_REFOUNDATION.md` (the pattern of CRED-9a to CRED-10, §5.14). 🔗 dep:STOR-14.

### 5.10 Legal facts register — immediately after N-TEST-1 (2026-10-05)

> **ℹ️ CARD [LEGAL-REGISTER] — first in the queue: it starts immediately after N-TEST-1 closes, before §5.9 and §5.11. Under way since 2026-10-07, after the closure of phase 0 of N-TEST-1 (2026-10-06), with the method below. The mechanics slice (L-0 to L-3) has been on `main` since that same day (#459 to #473): LEGAL-2, LEGAL-3 and LEGAL-4 closed and the skeleton of LEGAL-1; next come LEGAL-1's decision sessions, the retro pass by batches and LEGAL-5.**
> **The register, its place on the route and its method** — approved on 2026-10-04, placed on 2026-10-05 exactly after the Testing Refoundation, and the method of 2026-10-07 shared with §5.15: mechanics first, the present taken by the generators, one retro pass guided by the ledger below, the qualitative columns "pending decision" until the Product Owner's sessions, and the sequence L-0, L-1, L-2, L-3, the retro pass, LEGAL-5 — are [ADR-0042](../technical/ADR-0042-legal-facts-register-method.md). §5.15 follows it on the route, sharing the form of its gate (LEGAL-3 ↔ SUP-3) and the edits of the templates (LEGAL-2 ↔ SUP-2).
>
> - [~] **LEGAL-1** — the register, anchored in the GDPR record of processing activities (art. 30): personal data, purposes, legal bases and retention; subprocessors; OAuth permissions and data brought in over the network; security measures (for the credentials, ADR-0034, with its stance on a breach); commercial rules (ADR-0024 to ADR-0030). It also inventories the purposes with their legal basis, on which the destiny of `ConsentRecord` depends (`schema.prisma:3322-3336`, today with no reads or writes, with an entry in the guard and an RLS policy), which LEGAL-5 resolves (card C7 of §5.13; PREG-6, decided on 2026-10-06). Its qualitative columns —purposes, legal bases, retention periods, frameworks beyond the GDPR and the language of the register— stay "pending decision" until the Product Owner's sessions (ADR-0042, rule 4). **Progress 2026-10-07 (#465):** the register's skeleton, `docs/legal/REGISTER.md`, with the 13 sections of GDPR art. 30, each one `generated`, `linked` or `pending decision (Product Owner)`, and `docs/legal/README.md`. Waiting for the decision sessions: the pending sections, the frameworks beyond the GDPR, retention, the language of the register and 17 `pending` rows of the inventories (16 schema fields and one subprocessors dependency), which fitness #45 holds exact.
> - [x] **LEGAL-2** — a `## Legal impact` section in the ADR and specification templates; closed 2026-10-07 by #473 (merge `2fd9d33d`).
> - [x] **LEGAL-3** — the CI gate on legally relevant surfaces, `pnpm check:legal` as fitness #45; closed 2026-10-07 by #472 (merge `7b49a3b0`).
> - [x] **LEGAL-4** — the four inventories generated from the code, and their initial load; closed 2026-10-07 by #461 to #469 (merges `bb439662` to `f317a6e4`) and #471 (`24e56a53`); findings DEF-52 to DEF-62 (§5.11), registered by #460.
> - [ ] **LEGAL-5** — the acceptance evidence and the destiny of `ConsentRecord` (added on 2026-10-06, decision of PREG-6): (a) a versioned record of the acceptance of the terms, of the privacy notice and of the DPA —who, which version, when and from where— as contractual evidence, separate from any consent; (b) once LEGAL-1 has inventoried the purposes, `ConsentRecord` stays only for the purposes whose legal basis is consent (candidate: marketing email to individual creators), with the evidence the EDPB asks for (the text and version shown, the moment, the mechanism and the session; guidelines 05/2020, ¶108) and a withdrawal as easy as the opt-in; if there is none, it is deleted with its entry in the guard and its policy, and its row of card C7 (§5.13) is updated; (c) the sign-up never asks for a consent in the same click as the acceptance of the terms (¶81). 🔗 dep:LEGAL-1.
>

>
> The ledger grows by state, not by position in the phase: an item enters when it reaches `[x]` and its change touches data or credentials, whatever section it comes from (Leveling, product base, Pre-phase verdicts, defects or §5.x items); items of pure process or documentation (templates, gates, plan edits) do not enter (ADR-0042 decision 3, 2026-10-07), and the rule also applies to the rows already listed. An open item whose already merged slices touch credentials, personal data, OAuth, consent, retention or a third party also enters, as a "(partial)" row, so that what was published does not stay unread; its row is completed with the item's closing PR. An item that closes with the LEGAL-3 gate already on `main` enters with its two columns marked by its own closing PR, which the gate requires to document it (ADR-0042, rule 3).
>
> A row is marked in "Legal" only when its facts are in `docs/legal/`, and in "Support" only when its page is in `docs/support/`; an item without legal facts or without a support surface is marked with a declared not-applicable, with its reason, in the batch's PR. That declaration belongs to the ledger and is read by whoever reviews the batch, not by a gate: LEGAL-3's not-applicable lives in the inventories' classification files. The "Batch" column carries the number of the PR of the retro batch that marks the row, or that of the closing PR for an item closed with the gate already on `main`.
>
> From the Pre-phase, the verdicts whose text names personal data, credentials, OAuth, consent, retention or a third-party service enter: 8 verdicts in 4 rows (P-GATE-1, P-GATE-2 and six P.B documents grouped in two rows). Left out are 128 of the 136 verdicts that their blocks add up to (P.A 14, P.B 36, P.C 1, P.D 72, P.E 9 and P.F 4): the 14 of P.A, 30 of the 36 of P.B, the one of P.C, the 72 of P.D, the 9 of P.E, P-VERIFY-1 and P-VERIFY-2 (14 + 30 + 1 + 72 + 9 + 2 = 128) decide the destiny of a document or of an engram memory, or measure cycles and endpoints, without naming any of those surfaces; P.A-12 names authentication only as a false claim of the document that was deleted.

### 5.11 Defects found on 2026-10-04 — queued after N-TEST-1

> **ℹ️ CARD [DEFECTS-2026-10-04] — decided (item 3 of 2026-10-04); they do not start until N-TEST-1 closes.**
> The ones the payments and storage decisions absorb (F2, F3, F11) live in §5.9. These are fixed as decided:
>
> - [ ] **DEF-1** — F10: the Paddle webhook route wraps everything in a single `try` and answers 400 "Invalid signature" to any error; an invalid signature (400) is separated from a processing error (500, so the gateway retries), each with its own log entry.
> - [ ] **DEF-2** — an unconfigured gateway or OAuth provider answers 500 (`getAdapter` outside the `try`); it must answer 404/503 "not configured".
> - [ ] **DEF-3** — F8: Facebook's `client_secret` travels in the query of a URL (`providerOAuthConfigs.ts:295`, `facebook/src/apiClient.ts:237,252`); it moves to the body if Meta accepts it and, in any case, that URL never reaches a log.
> - [ ] **DEF-4** — Paddle's SDK compares the signature with `===`: OmniPost verifies the signature with `crypto.timingSafeEqual` and the SDK only parses the event; the issue for PaddleHQ is drafted and shown to the Maintainer before it is published.
> - [ ] **DEF-5** — fitness #16 does not see `process.env[...]` reads with brackets (12 price IDs): it is widened with its red path proven (SMELL-190).
> - [x] **DEF-6** — `any` on 3 lines of `quality/scripts/bundle-analyzer.ts`; closed 2026-10-11, when the file was deleted with DEF-84, by #553 (merge `47230884`).
>
> **Added on 2026-10-05** — found while sizing the §5.12 route migration (ADR-0033) and while verifying its findings, read statically, not run. They stay queued as findings: their fix is decided when they are taken, and those that live in §5.12 code name the area that moves it.
>
> - [ ] **DEF-7** — two customer routes read a principal their guard never sets: `apps/api/src/auth/apiKeyRoutes.ts:86` and `apps/api/src/custom-reports/customReportRoutes.ts:333` take the `accountId` from `request.user`, but their preHandler `requireClientAuth` only sets `request.customerUser` (`auth/customerAuthMiddleware.ts:59`; the only one that writes `request.user` is `auth/integrationAuthMiddleware.ts:175`). The `accountId` is `undefined` for every customer and every call answers 401. Neither the portals nor the tests use those routes, which is why nothing failed. Verified statically, not run. Same code as ARCH-17 and ARCH-18. SMELL-194. The `apiKeyRoutes.ts` half closes by deletion with DC-13 (§5.13, decision D4 of 2026-10-06); the `customReportRoutes.ts` half remains.
> - [ ] **DEF-8** — the account update from Admin stores the email unnormalized: `apps/api/src/admin/AnalyticsAccountHandlers.ts:64-84` checks uniqueness and writes the value as it arrives, and its schema (`admin/analyticsSchemas.ts:48`) does not normalize it, while account creation does (`accounts/accountRoutes.ts:154`). An email that differs only in case passes the route's uniqueness check and is stored that way. Verified statically, not run. Same code as ARCH-5 (AA-5). SMELL-195.
> - [ ] **DEF-9** — the OIDC PKCE state lives in process memory: `apps/api/src/auth/oidcRoutes.ts:63` keeps it in a module `Map`, and its own comment (`:59-60`) says that in production it must go in Redis or in encrypted cookies. With more than one pod, a login that starts on one and comes back to the callback through another does not find the state and fails. Fitness #14 does not see it: it only looks for class fields `private *Cache = new Map()`. Verified statically, not run. Same code as ARCH-17. SMELL-196. Fix decided on 2026-10-06 (ADR-0034, decision 7): the state moves to Redis with the `OAuthFlowStore` pattern and an atomic consume, CRED-3 (§5.14); the credentials inventory flagged it again and it has no row of its own.
> - [ ] **DEF-10** — updating notification preferences is not atomic: `apps/api/src/notifications/notificationRoutes.ts:291-293` runs one `upsert` per preference outside any Unit of Work, and a failure midway leaves the first ones applied and not the following ones. Verified statically, not run. Same code as ARCH-14. SMELL-197.
> - [ ] **DEF-11** — the onboarding GET writes: `apps/api/src/onboarding/onboardingRoutes.ts:37-44` creates the record if it does not exist, and completing a step (`:103-120`) runs two statements without a transaction. Verified statically, not run. Same code as ARCH-10. SMELL-198.
> - [ ] **DEF-12** — `TrendAnalysisService` receives `PrismaClient` and never uses it (`apps/api/src/trends/trendAnalysisService.ts:55-60`, no `this.prisma`), and it is the only reason `trendRoutes.ts:348` resolves `PrismaClient` from the container. In addition, `getTrendingContent` and `generateTrendPredictions` return `[]` behind a module circuit breaker (`:82`, `:153`), and the five endpoints of `trendRoutes.ts` have no references in the portals. Whether the service and those endpoints stay is the Product Owner's decision, under the §0.3 taxonomy. Verified statically, not run. Same code as ARCH-19. SMELL-199.
> - [ ] **DEF-13** — the Admin password-reset link falls back to `http://localhost:3100` when its base URL is missing: `apps/api/src/admin/adminUserRoutes.ts:398` and `apps/api/src/admin/auth/adminAuthRoutes.ts:263` evaluate `(adminUrlResult.ok && adminUrlResult.value) || "http://localhost:3100"` over `PlatformCredentialService.getCredential("PLATFORM", "adminUrl")`, a platform credential configured in Admin (`packages/core/settings/src/credentialKeys.ts:54`), not an environment variable. The link goes out the same way if the credential is not loaded, if it is empty or if its read or its decryption fails, so a store error stays hidden, and the email reaches the administrator with a link to `localhost` instead of the application. It is the class the environment canon forbids for `env` (SECURITY_CANON §Secrets and Environment: a typed value that fails fast, never `X || "fallback"`), here over a stored configuration that reaches users; fitness #15 only looks at `env` fallback values with a secret's name and does not see it. Verified statically, not run. Same code as ARCH-6. SMELL-200.
> - [ ] **DEF-14** — the CORS origin list the API uses is fixed to `localhost`: `apps/api/src/index.ts:391-400` passes `SecurityManager` six origins (`http` and `https` on ports 3000, 3100 and 3200) with `allowCredentials: true` (`:403`), and `SecurityManager.register` hands them as they are to `@fastify/cors` (`apps/api/src/security/securityHeaders.ts:149-155`). No environment value reaches that list and no branch depends on the environment, so every deployment, production included, admits credentialed calls from a browser only for those six `localhost` origins, and any other origin receives the response without `Access-Control-Allow-Origin`. The portals do not notice because they call the API from their servers (in Admin, a Server Action and a server layout; in both, the proxy route `app/api/backend/[...path]/route.ts`). It is the same class as DEF-13: a URL configuration fixed in code, with no typed environment value. Outside the §5.12 areas. Verified statically, not run. SMELL-201.

Nothing in `src` uses it (the API builds its own configuration in `index.ts:374`, DEF-14); only two unit suites that pin its values import it (`apps/api/tests/unit/securityHeaders.test.ts:137`, `apps/api/tests/unit/securityHeaders.mutations.test.ts:505`), and that is why it looks like tested configuration. In the same file, `handleCorsOrigin` (`:168`) has no callers: `register` hands the array to `@fastify/cors` (`:149-155`), so its matching of `*` and `*.domain` never runs. > - [ ] **DEF-16** — the API scheduler loses the context of its errors: `apps/api/src/infrastructure/container/setupServices.ts:163` passes it `createLogger("scheduler")`, a pino logger, as `SchedulerLogger`, whose `error(message, data)` puts the message first (`packages/observability/background-scheduler/src/port.ts:17-21`). Pino reads `(message, object)` as a message plus a format argument and drops the object when the message has no specifier, so the error of a scheduled task is logged without `taskId` or `err` (`packages/observability/background-scheduler/src/default-scheduler.ts:139-142`, `:168-171` and `:175-178`; also the `info` at `:144`). Measured with the repository's pino (10.3.1): that call writes only the message. The workers wrap theirs in pino's order (`apps/workers/src/publishWorker.ts:134-140`, `apps/workers/src/bootstrap.ts:74-80`). It is the risk class that decision D-R10 of ADR-0033 closed for the core; the rest, verified statically.

A sweep that could not delete reads as a clean one with zero rows, and no WARN or ERROR records it: the service logs no failure and the daily task discards its result (`apps/api/src/index.ts:974-981`). In addition, a failure to read the GDPR settings is taken as automatic deletion turned off and returns zeros without auditing anything (`:37-41`). Item (v) only removed the closing `info` line. The daily task discards both results (`apps/api/src/index.ts:963-964`), so no failure reaches a log. Verified statically, not run.

While one of those connections fails, nothing logs it; CODING_STANDARDS §Error Handling admits no empty `catch`, and an empty error handler is its equivalent. The two at `setupServices.ts:849` and `:870` stay out of the count: their comment says where each failure goes (the fail-open path of the brute-force adapter and the `err("STORE_ERROR")` of the MFA challenge store). > - [x] **DEF-27** — the click-count integration test was intermittent; closed 2026-10-14 by #574 (merge `b5773184`).
> - [ ] **DEF-28** — recording a click and its GA4 event can fail without anything logging it: `RedirectAndTrackClickUseCase` fires `recordClick` with `void` (`packages/core/links/src/RedirectAndTrackClickUseCase.ts:65`; its comment at `:64` justifies it by performance) and the event with `void this.ga4?.trackEvent(...)` (`:68`; the one at `:67`, because a failure must not hold up the redirect), and observes neither result. The port declares `recordClick` as `Promise<Result<void, Error>>` (`packages/core/domain/src/repositories/TrackedLinkRepository.ts:67`, not in `packages/ports`), and the Prisma adapter does the write —the `linkClick.create` and the increment of `trackedLink.clicks`, in a transaction (`apps/api/src/infrastructure/repositories/PrismaTrackedLinkRepository.ts:215-237`)— inside a `try` that turns any failure into `err(...)` (`:211`, `:240-242`): what the `void` discards is an error `Result` already resolved, not a rejected promise, and a click that was not saved is lost without a log line. In GA4 the loss is also in the wiring: its adapter always returns `ok` (`apps/api/src/infrastructure/adapters/GA4TrackingAdapter.ts:99-100`), as its port asks (`packages/core/domain/src/repositories/GA4TrackingPort.ts:26-28`, `:35`), and logs its failures (`:89-97`) with a logger the root does not pass it (`apps/api/src/infrastructure/container/setupServices.ts:894`), so it uses the empty default (`GA4TrackingAdapter.ts:35-39`). It is the class of DEF-17 and DEF-18: a port failure turned into silence. Proposed fix: the redirect still does not wait, but both promises are observed; the use case receives a `LoggerPort` (`packages/ports/src/LoggerPort.ts`) that the composition root injects, as in the five services of ADR-0033, and logs a `warn` with `err` and `trackedLinkId` when `recordClick` returns an error or rejects, and the same for the GA4 event, which only stops being lost if the root also passes a logger to its adapter, because the adapter always returns `ok`; no `void` over an unobserved `Result`. The deterministic seam of DEF-27 and the observation point of DEF-28 are in the same place (`:65`), so a single change can close both. Verified statically, not run. It waits its turn in this queue, like the rest of §5.11.

> **Added on 2026-10-05 from the verification of F1** — DEF-29, confirmed at run time; unlike the previous ones, the Product Owner decided to fix it now as a unit of its own, outside the chain of slice 0.22, because it breaks publishing.
>
> - [~] **DEF-29** — the workers cannot decrypt the channel credentials the API writes: the API encrypts `Channel.credentials` with AES-256-GCM and binds `Channel.credentials\x1f<channelId>` as AAD (`apps/api/src/security/EncryptionService.ts:99-101` and `:152`; `apps/api/src/security/ChannelCredentialsCrypto.ts:40` and `:65-69`, called from `PrismaChannelRepository.save` `:348-351`), and the seed does the same (`infra/prisma/seed.ts:48-72`); the workers decrypt with `packages/shared/src/channelCredentialsCrypto.ts:79-102`, which never calls `setAAD` nor reads `credentialsKeyVersion`, through the callback of `packages/adapters/db-prisma/src/ChannelRepository.ts:83-90`, which receives the four envelope columns and not the channel id (`apps/workers/src/publishWorker.ts:127-132`, `apps/workers/src/mentionIngestWorker.ts:71-79`). Measured on 2026-10-05 with the real modules and a throwaway key: the envelope written by the API throws `Decryption failed: data may be tampered or key version is wrong` in the worker, and with the AAD it decrypts; the full path returns `err(DATABASE_ERROR)` in `getChannelsByIds` and `err(AUTH)` in `CredentialResolver.resolve`. In production every publish fails with `AUTH` (`apps/workers/src/publishHandler.ts:282-305`) and mention ingestion marks `needsReauth` on healthy channels (`apps/workers/src/lib/handleProviderAuthError.ts:28-37`); reconnecting encrypts with AAD again, so the failure repeats. The divergence starts in `1a48bbf3` (2026-05-03), whose JSDoc claims that the shared helper mirrors `EncryptionService` and that the API, the workers and the seed use it; neither was true from that commit on. The only test of the worker path with real encryption, `apps/api/tests/integration/publishWorkerTenantIsolation.integration.test.ts:122` and `:143-145`, encrypts and decrypts with the same helper without AAD, which is why it stayed green. Second latent defect: the helper ignores `credentialsKeyVersion`, so after a rotation the workers would have no previous key to look up. **PR 1 merged, PR 2 pending** (2026-10-06): the DEF-29 unit fixes it in two PRs. PR 1 —one canonical cipher with AAD and a key ring per version in the Node module of the shared core, the channel id in the adapter contract, the workers with `PLATFORM_ENCRYPTION_KEY_VERSION` and `_V<N>`— was merged into `main` on 2026-10-05 (#429, `8710327d`); PR 2 —`EncryptionService` and the seed delegate to it, a cross-process test, the `CREDENTIALS_UNREADABLE` code end to end and an opaque key ring— is pending, goes after unit B (DEF-37, merged on 2026-10-06 as #434 and #435) and its number is assigned when it is published. ADR-0034, which that PR was going to write in a narrow form ("one cipher for channel credentials"), was written on 2026-10-06 as the full credentials model (§5.14, #433): PR 2 cites it instead of writing it. The rows already written are not re-encrypted: they carry the AAD and the active version, and the fixed worker reads them. The fix only unblocks Bluesky: measured with the cipher of PR 1, the eight OAuth providers still do not publish because of DEF-36. The box is checked when PR 2 is on `main`.

Only `apps/api/tests/unit/auditMiddleware.test.ts` calls the audit function, and it calls it directly, so no test noticed. The metrics half is repaired inside card C1 (the Maintainer's decision of 2026-10-05, unit DC-1 of §5.13); the audit half changes behavior (rows per request) and goes here. Proposed fix: the audit hook runs for every authenticated route, with a test over the composed app that proves it; the 401s and 403s of `requirePermission` are recorded (it picks up the intention of `auditPermissionAccess`, which leaves with card C2); volume and retention reviewed. Verified statically, not run. Same code as ARCH-21. **Finding while measuring DEF-70 (2026-10-09):** once it runs, its classification does not work either. `getActionFromRequest` (`audit/auditMiddleware.ts:112-171`) decides the action with prefixes over the raw `request.url`, the class of DEF-68, and not over the route pattern: the `/publish/` branch (`:161`) matches no registered route, and `POST_CREATED` (`:156`) requires that a path starting with `/posts` contain no `/`, so it is never chosen. `apps/api/tests/unit/auditMiddleware.test.ts:248-258` calls the function with `/publish/123` and only checks that a record exists. The fix of DEF-30 includes classifying by `request.routeOptions.url` and method, with a test that requires every branch to match a registered route, like the one of DEF-70. It waits its turn in this queue, like the rest of §5.11.

> - [ ] **DEF-32** — analytics ingestion persists nothing for any of the 11 networks and marks healthy channels for reconnection. Confirmed at run time on 2026-10-05 on `main` `20c4e9ea`, with a read-only harness (the real use case and adapters, mocked API clients and repositories, no database): `IngestChannelAnalyticsUseCase` reads `metrics` as an array of daily rows, but Facebook, Instagram, LinkedIn, Pinterest, Snapchat, TikTok, X and YouTube return an object (`FacebookAdapter.ts:472`, `InstagramAdapter.ts:550`, `LinkedInAdapter.ts:342`, `PinterestAdapter.ts:330`, `SnapchatAdapter.ts:318`, `TikTokAdapter.ts:387`, `XAdapter.ts:424`, `YouTubeAdapter.ts:281`), so `metrics.map` (`packages/core/analytics/src/IngestChannelAnalyticsUseCase.ts:144`) throws inside the transaction, before `upsertDailySummaries`, and the use case answers `INTERNAL_ERROR` (`:181-188`) without writing a row; Telegram returns no `metrics` and ends in `ok` with zero rows, without a warning; Threads returns an array, but its `postId` is the Threads media id while `AnalyticsDailySummary.postId` is a foreign key to `Post.id` (migration `20260502010535`), so it would fail with P2003 (read in the code); Bluesky does not implement `fetchAnalytics` (`ANALYTICS_NOT_SUPPORTED`). In production that step is not even reached: `PrismaChannelRepository.toDomain` passes the credentials through `parseCredentials` (`apps/api/src/infrastructure/repositories/PrismaChannelRepository.ts:53-61`), which keeps only `accessToken`, `refreshToken`, `expiresAt`, `tokenType` and `scope`; the `REQUIRED_FIELDS` of each adapter requires a field of its own network (`pageId`, `userId`, `personUrn`, `boardId`, `clientId`, `botToken`, `clientKey`, `apiKey`…), `validateCredentialStructure` fails with `err("AUTH")`, the use case answers `AUTH_ERROR`, `apps/api/src/analytics/analyticsIngestConsumer.ts:97` marks `needsReauth` ("Provider rejected credentials during analytics ingestion") and throws (`:107`), and BullMQ retries three times: every channel of the 10 networks with `fetchAnalytics` is marked for reconnection every 6 h and nothing is persisted; on reconnecting, `PrismaChannelRepository.save` (`:333-347`) re-encrypts only the five generic fields. That chain was read in the code for ingestion; the same projection was confirmed at run time in DEF-37 and DEF-38. The readers that see nothing: custom reports, repurpose detection, the top-performers context and the trend score; `AnalyticsMonthlySummary` is read and never written. No test joins the real output of an adapter or the credentials the repository loads with the use case: the unit test feeds a hand-made array with a mocked adapter and channel (`apps/api/tests/unit/application/ingestChannelAnalytics.test.ts:44-49`). The shapes never matched (the use case was born in `8e5f8f5a`, on 2026-03-30, expecting an array; Facebook returns an object since `5603d6be`); the projection is from `5603d6be`, the dispatcher from `7679830d` (2026-05-11) and the in-process consumer from `ff675659` (2026-05-24). Root of the contract: the port returns `unknown` (`packages/ports/src/ProviderAdapter.ts:162-165`), and F2-API-12 types it. Its two leads were confirmed at run time the same day: the comment use cases receive the same projected credentials (DEF-38), and the OAuth connection flow saves only generic fields, so it does not publish either (DEF-36). Proposed fix: the projection keeps each network's own fields, the `fetchAnalytics` contract is typed (F2-API-12) and an integration test joins the real adapter, the credentials the repository loads and the use case; a projection defect never marks `needsReauth`. It waits its turn in this queue, like the rest of §5.11.

> **Added on 2026-10-05 while verifying the leads of DEF-32 at run time** — DEF-36 to DEF-38 share one cause: the channel credentials model. Application keys are asked for per channel instead of coming from the configuration, the per-channel ids (page, board, organization) are not discovered on connecting and a repository trims the credentials to five generic fields. Writer and reader diverge since `1a48bbf3` (2026-05-03), the same commit as DEF-29, and the OAuth flow saves three generic fields since `f9d64a40` (2026-05-07); no test joins a row written by the OAuth flow with a real adapter.
>
> - [ ] **DEF-36** — channels connected by OAuth cannot publish: the OAuth callback of the 8 OAuth providers stores in `Channel.credentials` only `{accessToken, refreshToken?, expiresAt}` (`apps/api/src/auth/providerOAuthFlow.ts:193-197`); the provider's id goes only to the `providerAccountId` column (`:202`), there is no page, board or organization selection, and reconnecting (`:216-225`) writes the same three fields; `POST /channels` and `PUT /channels/:channelId` keep only the five generic fields with `mapCredentials` (`apps/api/src/channels/channelRoutes.ts:73-81`). Each adapter requires in `REQUIRED_FIELDS` application keys or per-channel ids that nobody stores (X: `apiKey`, `apiSecret` and `bearerToken`, `XAdapter.ts:43`; Facebook: `pageId`, `appId` and `appSecret`, `:45`; Instagram: `userId`, `:52`; YouTube: `clientId`, `clientSecret` and `channelId`, `:45`; TikTok: `clientKey`, `clientSecret` and `openId`, `:46`; LinkedIn: `personUrn`, `:44`; Pinterest: `boardId`, `:46`; Snapchat: `clientId`, `clientSecret` and `organizationId`, `:40`), and the workers' `CredentialResolver` (`apps/workers/src/services/CredentialResolver.ts:58`) hands over the decrypted JSON without merging `providerAccountId` or the Admin `SOCIAL_*` keys (`packages/core/settings/src/credentialKeys.ts:60-70`, which nobody reads). Confirmed at run time on 2026-10-05 with the real publish handler and the real adapters over mocked HTTP: with the DEF-29 encryption applied, the 8 OAuth providers end in `PublishLog` ERR `AUTH` with zero calls to the provider; only Bluesky publishes, and the seed channel `dev-x` fails with `AUTH`; Telegram and Threads have no connection writer (enum of `providerOAuthFlow.ts:32`, configurations of `providerOAuthConfigs.ts:644-646`). Mention ingestion marks `needsReauth` on the Facebook, Instagram and X channels. Proposed fix: the connection flows store the per-channel fields each adapter requires, discovered on connecting; the application keys come from the configuration when credentials are resolved; the repository stops trimming; a credentials contract typed per provider replaces the `REQUIRED_FIELDS` lists; contract tests join a real row of the OAuth flow with the real adapter. It is the first slice of the §2F workstream (write path per provider, [`docs/archive/PENDING_WORK_INVENTORY.md` §2F](https://github.com/edwardricardo/omni-post/blob/cb4921bda93a39e300f1bd5203985edde8055258/docs/archive/PENDING_WORK_INVENTORY.md#L198), which was deleted from the repository on 2026-10-17 and git history keeps), not a one-night unit. **Decided on 2026-10-05 (option B):** the surgical part (DEF-37) goes now as a unit of its own, the credentials model went into ADR-0034 (2026-10-06) and this redesign is slice 1 of §2F, CRED-14 (§5.14), which waits for DEF-37 and CRED-8a; before building, the Maintainer asked for the inventory of every credential in the environment and the database, so that the unit would not stay isolated from them.
> - [x] **DEF-37** — saving a Bluesky channel destroyed its credentials; closed 2026-10-06 by #434 (merge `e0b2cee0`) and #435 (merge `5a65dba0`).
> - [ ] **DEF-38** — comment sync and reply do not work: with the same credential projection, `SyncProviderCommentsUseCase` returns `FORBIDDEN` for LinkedIn and the consumer marks `needsReauth` in every 30-minute cycle, on every channel not deleted (`processInboxSyncJob` → `UpdateChannelAuthState`); for Facebook, Instagram, X and YouTube the sync does nothing and reports success, because the use case never passes `postExternalId` and the adapters return `[]` before calling; `SendReplyUseCase` fails with `AUTH` on the five providers that support replies (Facebook, Instagram, LinkedIn and YouTube before calling; X after an unauthenticated call), and Pinterest, TikTok and Snapchat do not support replies. Confirmed at run time with the real use cases (`packages/core/inbox/src/SyncProviderCommentsUseCase.ts`, `SendReplyUseCase.ts`) and mocked repositories. Proposed fix: it depends on DEF-36 (complete credentials) and adds the `postExternalId` in the sync, with per-provider tests over the real use case. It waits its turn with slice 1 of §2F, CRED-14 (§5.14).
