> **Added on 2026-10-06 by the credentials inventory (§5.14), registered in #433** — DEF-39 to DEF-45, measured on `main` `8710327d` by reading the code, not run; the Maintainer decided the fix of each one with the credentials model (ADR-0034), and it lives in a row of §5.14 or, for DEF-44, of §5.13. The in-memory OIDC PKCE state, which the inventory also flags, was already DEF-9. They wait their turn in this queue, like the previous ones.
>
> - [ ] **DEF-39** — the Admin and customer TOTP seeds are stored in plain text: `AdminUser.mfaSecret` (`infra/prisma/schema.prisma:186`) and `CustomerUser.mfaSecret` (`:357`) are written as they come out of `authenticator.generateSecret()` (`apps/api/src/admin/auth/MfaService.ts:120-126`, through `apps/api/src/infrastructure/adapters/PrismaAdminMfaUserRepository.ts:71` and `PrismaCustomerMfaUserRepository.ts:76`) and are read to verify each code (`MfaService.ts:160-162`, `:209-211`). A read of the database hands over the second factor of every administrator and every user; NIST SP 800-63B-4 §3.1.4.2 asks that those shared keys be strongly protected against disclosure, and they cannot be hashed because verification needs them. Fix decided on 2026-10-06 (ADR-0034, decision 6): encrypt them with the single encryption seam and delete the plain-text column, CRED-15a (§5.14). Verified statically, not run.
> - [ ] **DEF-40** — the HubSpot and Salesforce OAuth tokens are stored in plain text: `CrmConnection.accessToken` and `refreshToken` (`schema.prisma:2853-2854`, `@db.Text`) are sent by the client in the request body (`apps/api/src/crm/crmRoutes.ts:35-36`, `:96-97`), written unencrypted by `apps/api/src/infrastructure/repositories/PrismaCrmConnectionRepository.ts:60-72` and read by `packages/core/crm/src/SyncCrmContactsUseCase.ts:70`; the API read does mask them (`packages/core/crm/src/GetCrmConnectionsQuery.ts:36-40`). It is the same class of secret as `Channel.credentials`, which is encrypted with AAD and a key version. There is no server-side code exchange either: the portal sends the tokens, and which application connects the CRM stays an open point of ADR-0034. Fix decided on 2026-10-06 (ADR-0034, decisions 6 and 11): encrypt them with the single encryption seam, with a write-only API, CRED-15b (§5.14). Verified statically, not run.
> - [ ] **DEF-41** — the password-reset and invitation tokens are stored in plain text and looked up by equality: `AdminUser.passwordResetToken` (`schema.prisma:183`, written by `apps/api/src/admin/auth/PasswordService.ts:192-203`, looked up and claimed at `:251-258` and `:322-330`), `CustomerUser.resetToken` (`:354`, written from `packages/core/customer-auth/src/RequestPasswordResetUseCase.ts:108` and claimed in `apps/api/src/infrastructure/repositories/PrismaCustomerUserRepository.ts:222-226`) and `CustomerUser.inviteToken` (`:367`, written from `packages/core/team/src/InviteTeamMemberUseCase.ts:79`). Whoever reads the database can use any valid token: a random token stored in clear and compared by equality is a password in clear (OWASP Forgot Password; NIST SP 800-63B-4 §3.1.2.2). The `"CHANGE_REQUIRED"` sentinel that `apps/api/src/admin/AccountSessionService.ts:79` writes into the same column (SMELL-110) first moves to a flag of its own. It shares the claim sites of `PasswordService` with N-SEC-6 (§1) and the consumption markers of fitness #41, which are renamed with the column. Fix decided on 2026-10-06 (ADR-0034, decision 6): `@unique` columns with the SHA-256 hash of the token, looked up by hash through a single `tokenHash()` helper, CRED-15c (§5.14). Verified statically, not run.
> - [ ] **DEF-42** — the workers and the provider packages log without redaction, and the API's list has gaps: `@observability/logger`, the logger of `apps/workers` and of every package, is a `pino()` with no `redact` option (`packages/observability/logger/src/index.ts:30-38`), and the workers hold the decrypted credentials in memory; the API's `REDACT_PATHS` (`apps/api/src/lib/logger.ts:14-35`) does not include `appPassword` (Bluesky), `clientSecret`, `appSecret`, `botToken`, `secretKey`, `webhookSecret`, `mfaSecret`, `backupCodes`, `codeVerifier`, `webhookUrl`, `targetUrl` or `privateKey`, and its wildcards only cover `password`, `token`, `apiKey` and `secret` at one level (not `*.accessToken`, `*.refreshToken` or `*.credentials`). A sweep found no call today that logs a credentials object, but nothing prevents it. Fix decided on 2026-10-06 (ADR-0034, decision 8): one exported list that every logger applies, CRED-2 (§5.14). Verified statically, not run.
> - [ ] **DEF-43** — the keys of the platform AI pool are read from two sources: the Admin orchestrator takes `OPENAI_API_KEY`, `PERPLEXITY_API_KEY` and `GEMINI_API_KEY` from the environment (`apps/api/src/ai/orchestrator.ts:116-157`, for the health checks and the requests without an `accountId`), and customer requests read the `AI_POOL` group edited in Admin (`packages/core/ai/src/AiRequestService.ts:109`). An operator can change a key in Admin while the orchestrator keeps the one from the environment, or the other way round. Fix decided on 2026-10-06 (ADR-0034, decision 2): Admin is the single source and the three variables leave the environment, CRED-8b (§5.14). Verified statically, not run.
> - [ ] **DEF-44** — the API does not start without a dead key: `OAUTH_ENCRYPTION_KEY` is required in the environment schema (`apps/api/src/config/env.ts:119`, `z.string().min(SECRET_MIN)`) and no code reads it (`rg -n 'OAUTH_ENCRYPTION_KEY' apps packages` only finds the schema and the catalog); its table, `ProviderConnection`, was deleted with the migration `20260507222903_consolidate_channel_drop_provider_connection`. Every deployment has to generate and safeguard a secret that protects nothing, and `docs/security/SECRETS.md` and `secretCatalog.ts:75-79` describe it as the KEK of `ProviderConnection`. Fix: DC-3 deletes it (§5.13, card C3), already decided; ADR-0034 (decision 13) counts it among the dead secrets. Verified statically, not run.
> - [ ] **DEF-45** — key rotation exists only on paper: there is no re-encryption job, and the error at `apps/api/src/security/EncryptionService.ts:241` points the operator to a "re-wrap script" that does not exist; two rotation records drive nothing —`PlatformEncryptionKey` (`schema.prisma:3572`), which the Admin rotation endpoint fills with a new version and the instruction to edit `.env` and re-encrypt (`apps/api/src/settings/settingsRoutes.ts:156-175` → `SettingsService.logEncryptionKeyRotation`) without anything reading it to choose a key, and `SecretRotationLog` (`:290`), which has no run-time writer and was seeded by the migration `20260506193101_add_secret_rotation_log`, so the status page always shows the same date—; and the dual-key window for the JWT and cookie signing keys that the runbook prescribes (`docs/security/T0A_SECRETS_ROTATION_RUNBOOK.md`, "Dual-key validity windows") is not implemented: with no `kid` and no previous key, a rotation closes every session. Fix decided on 2026-10-06 (ADR-0034, decision 9): the re-encryption job with a single record (CRED-6) and the dual-key window (CRED-11), in §5.14. Verified statically, not run.

> **Added on 2026-10-06 by the audit-floors unit (#431)** — DEF-46, a dependency defect that unit measured that night and leaves outside its scope because it waits for a decision of the Maintainer; it waits its turn in this queue, like the previous ones.
>
> - [ ] **DEF-46** — `@sentry/nextjs` and `@sentry/node` are installed at 10.48.0 (catalog, `pnpm-workspace.yaml:136-137`), below 10.75.2, which fixes GHSA-mchm-gjx6-7h55 (a middleware bypass through the `tunnelRoute` option, `<=10.75.1`). The notice exists only as an advisory of Sentry's repository —OSV and GitHub's global endpoint answer 404—, so `pnpm audit` does not see it and the gate stays green over it. It is not reachable today: no portal (`apps/admin`, `apps/client`) configures `tunnelRoute`. The raise is deliberately not taken: Sentry 10.54.0 and later ship their own copies of the OpenTelemetry instrumentations, and those copies still emit `db.user` in the database spans (read in 10.75.3 and 11.4.0), so raising would turn the audit green and reopen the GHSA-qqmp-wf37-98f9 leak that the overrides of that same unit close. Decision pending from the Maintainer: (a) raise with a measured mitigation that removes `db.user` from the spans, or (b) wait until Sentry's copies bring the fix. Cross-reference: `docs/security/SECURITY_CANON.md` §"Blocked floors" (the entry was written by the audit-floors PR, #431, merged on 2026-10-06). Proposed fix: path (a), with the mitigation measured with the adapter-pg probe. Verified statically.

> **Added on 2026-10-06 by the credentials inventory and by the reading of unit B, registered in #433** — DEF-47, the exposure CRED-12 fixes, and DEF-48, found while writing unit B (DEF-37, #434) and outside its scope; both read in the code, not run. They wait their turn in this queue, like the previous ones.
>
> - [ ] **DEF-47** — the external-notifications list returns the Slack and Teams webhook URLs decrypted: `GET /external-notifications` (`apps/api/src/external-notifications/externalNotificationRoutes.ts:215-218`, with `requireClientAuth`) answers with `ListExternalNotificationsQuery`, which projects `webhookUrl` as it comes out of the repository (`packages/core/external-notifications/src/ListExternalNotificationsQuery.ts:49`), and the repository decrypts it on reading (`apps/api/src/infrastructure/repositories/PrismaExternalNotificationConfigRepository.ts:166-176`). It is a capability URL: whoever has it posts in the customer's Slack or Teams channel. At rest it is encrypted (`ExternalNotificationConfig.webhookUrl*`, `infra/prisma/schema.prisma:2515-2518`, with AAD per row), but every read of the list hands it over whole, and `REDACT_PATHS` does not cover `webhookUrl` (DEF-42). It is the only measured violation of the write-only rule of ADR-0034 (decision 10): a secret is shown once, when it is created, and masked afterwards. Proposed fix: the list returns the presence or the masked URL, never the decrypted value, with a route test; it is CRED-12 (§5.14). Verified statically, not run.
> - [ ] **DEF-48** — updating a channel overwrites its lifecycle fields: `PUT /channels/:channelId` (`apps/api/src/channels/channelRoutes.ts:663-670`) reaches `updateChannel` (`:370`), which rebuilds the channel with `Channel.reconstitute` (`:388-401`) passing only project, account, provider, name, credentials, status, error count, last error, last check and dates, without `isPrimary`, `needsReauth`, `authFailedAt`, `authFailureReason`, `accountName`, `profileImage`, `connectedAt`, `expiredAt`, `lastUsedAt` or `providerAccountId`; the update branch of the upsert of `PrismaChannelRepository.save` (`apps/api/src/infrastructure/repositories/PrismaChannelRepository.ts:380-395`) then writes them as `false` or `null`. Every rename or credentials change removes the primary-channel flag, erases a pending reauthentication flag, erases the expiry trail and the display data, and nulls `providerAccountId`, with which the OAuth callback finds the channel on reconnecting (`findByProjectProviderAccount`, `apps/api/src/auth/providerOAuthFlow.ts:205`): a reconnection after a rename creates a duplicate channel. Proposed fix: the handler loads the aggregate and applies its methods (`updateHandle` and `updateCredentials`, `packages/core/domain/src/entities/Channel.ts:368`, `:382`) instead of reconstituting it from the request, as the aggregates rule of ARCHITECTURE_CANON asks (state changes only through methods), with a route test that checks `isPrimary` and `providerAccountId` after a `PUT`; a unit of its own, ordered with slice 1 of §2F (CRED-14), because it is the same route family as DEF-36 and DEF-37. Verified statically, not run (engram 1335).
>
> **Added on 2026-10-06 by the fix of Tailwind's `@source` directive** — DEF-49, found by the `play` assertions of the `packages/ui` stories of PR E of slice 0.22 and fixed in its own branch (#444), and DEF-50, measured by whoever wrote that fix and outside its scope, because it waits for a decision of the Product Owner on the colors.
>
> - [x] **DEF-49** — no portal scanned `packages/ui` with Tailwind; fixed by #444 (merge `06fead38`), in `main` since 2026-10-06.
> - [ ] **DEF-50** — the Admin theme does not define the colors the shared components use: its `@theme inline` block (`apps/admin/app/globals.css:20`) defines `--color-muted-foreground`, but not `secondary`, `secondary-foreground`, `muted`, `popover`, `popover-foreground`, `card` or `card-foreground`, which the client does define (`apps/client/app/globals.css:32-48`), so Tailwind never generates in Admin the utilities that name them. Outside stories and tests, `packages/ui/src` uses `bg-secondary` and its variants 18 times, `bg-muted` and `border-muted` 12, `bg-popover` 5 and `bg-card` 4, plus `text-secondary-foreground` (2), `text-popover-foreground` (5) and `text-card-foreground` (1). Admin renders `@packages/ui` components that use them: the webhooks page (`apps/admin/app/[locale]/(dashboard)/webhooks/page.tsx:11-26`) imports `Tabs`, `Select` and `Card`, and their `TabsList` (`bg-muted`, `:177`), `SelectContent` (`bg-popover`, `:116`) and `Card` (`bg-card`, `:208`) are drawn without a background. It is not a path problem: the fix of DEF-49 does not change it. Measured on 2026-10-06 by whoever wrote that fix, and the tokens and the counts again on `main` `73a03dea`. Proposed fix: the Product Owner decides the colors, because Admin's own tokens (`--bg-*`, `--text-*`, `--accent*`) have no equivalent for those four; the `@theme inline` block defines them, with a test that fails when a color `packages/ui` uses has no variable in a portal's theme. It waits its turn in this queue, like the rest of §5.11.

> **Added on 2026-10-06 by the closure of phase 0 of N-TEST-1** — DEF-51, measured while raising the Storybook family to 10.6.1 (#447) and outside the scope of that closure; it waits its turn in this queue, like the previous ones.
>
> - [ ] **DEF-51** — `storybook build` of `apps/client` prints 58 warnings, the same in 10.6.0 and in 10.6.1 (measured on 2026-10-06): 48 Vite `MODULE_LEVEL_DIRECTIVE` for the `"use client"` directive of `packages/ui` components; 8 from react-docgen-typescript, which leaves `packages/ui` components and `.storybook/preview.tsx` out of the active TypeScript project ("not included in the active TypeScript project"), so their props are not documented by types (the autodocs pages no longer exist: `@storybook/addon-docs` left on 2026-10-25 with deps-remove-2); 1 "No story files found" for the glob `../components/**/*.stories.@(ts|tsx)` of `apps/client/.storybook/main.ts`, which today finds no file because no component of `apps/client/components` has its story beside it yet (that glob is the location ADR-0035 sets, and the warning goes away with the first story of the `apps/client` slice of the backfill); and 1 chunk-size warning. The same warnings come out in the smoke test (`storybook dev --smoke-test --ci`) and in `pnpm --filter @apps/client test:stories`. No gate fails because of them, but the zero-warnings rule reaches them. Proposed fix: a small configuration unit after the closure of phase 0 that brings them to zero, each one by its cause.

> **Added on 2026-10-07 by the cookie inventory of the legal register (§5.10, unit L-1b)** — DEF-52 and DEF-53, found while classifying the lifetime of each cookie from the code; they are registered and wait their turn, with no fix in that unit.
>
> - [ ] **DEF-52** — the API's `refreshToken` cookie lives about 7000 days instead of 7: `apps/api/src/auth/authRoutes.ts:112` and `:184`, `apps/api/src/auth/samlRoutes.ts:391` and `apps/api/src/auth/oidcRoutes.ts:464` write it with `maxAge: 7 * 24 * 60 * 60 * 1000`, a value in milliseconds that carries the comment «7 days» in `authRoutes.ts`, while `@fastify/cookie` 11.0.2 (`apps/api/package.json:118`, the same in `pnpm-lock.yaml`) reads `maxAge` in seconds —its README says so, and the serializer `cookie@1.1.1` writes it as is as `Max-Age`—, so the browser keeps the cookie 604 800 000 seconds, about 7000 days. The server-side expiry of the refresh token still bounds the session; the defect is that the cookie's own lifetime no longer backs it and the browser carries a dead cookie for years, with the `/auth` path and the token's value. Measured on 2026-10-07 by whoever wrote L-1b, and the four sites and the version again on this branch. Proposed fix: `maxAge: 7 * 24 * 60 * 60` in the four sites and a test that asserts `Max-Age=604800` in the `Set-Cookie` header of each login flow; a unit of its own, with its tests. It waits its turn in this queue, like the rest of §5.11.
> - [ ] **DEF-53** — Admin's backend proxy renews the `admin-session` cookie with a 1-day lifetime and skips the 15-minute helper: `apps/admin/lib/auth/sessionCookie.ts:31` sets `SESSION_MAX_AGE = 15 * 60` and declares itself the single source of that cookie, but `apps/admin/app/api/backend/[...path]/route.ts:46-52` writes it with `cookieStore.set("admin-session", …, { maxAge: 24 * 60 * 60 })` without going through `setSessionCookie`, so after a renewal through the proxy the short-lived JWT access token stays in the browser 96 times longer than the helper sets. The route `apps/admin/app/api/auth/refresh/route.ts`, by contrast, does use the helper, so two renewal paths leave the same cookie with different lifetimes. Measured on 2026-10-07 by whoever wrote L-1b. Proposed fix: the proxy calls `setSessionCookie` so that a single place owns the cookie's lifetime, with a test that fails when a write of `admin-session` does not go through the helper; a unit of its own. It waits its turn in this queue, like the rest of §5.11.
>
> **Added on 2026-10-07 by the OAuth permissions inventory of the legal register (§5.10, unit L-1c)** — DEF-54 to DEF-62, found while comparing the product's four scope declarations (connection configuration, adapters, shared metadata and CRM routes); they are registered and wait their turn, with no fix in that unit. Three scopes from a single source were reviewed and are benign: `x:offline.access`, `facebook:pages_show_list` and the read of Snapchat's `me.email` under `snapchat-marketing-api`.
>
> - [ ] **DEF-54** — Instagram: the connection flow never requests `instagram_content_publish`. `packages/providers/instagram/src/InstagramAdapter.ts:74` (`requiredScopes`) and `packages/shared/src/providers/providerConfig.ts:122` declare it, and `InstagramApiClient` publishes through `/{userId}/media` and `/{userId}/media_publish`, but `apps/api/src/auth/providerOAuthConfigs.ts:237` requests only `user_profile`, `user_media` and `instagram_basic`. Impact: a connected Instagram account stores a token without the publish permission. Proposed fix: request the scope in the connection configuration and add a test that asserts that every scope an adapter requires is requested by its connection configuration (the inventory's Mismatches section is the oracle); a unit of its own, with its tests. It waits its turn in this queue, like the rest of §5.11.
> - [ ] **DEF-55** — Instagram: the connection flow uses the retired Basic Display API. `apps/api/src/auth/providerOAuthConfigs.ts:237` sends `user_profile` and `user_media` to `api.instagram.com/oauth/authorize` (`:238`) and the connection step reads `graph.instagram.com/me` (`:263`), while the adapter works over the Graph API at `graph.facebook.com`; Meta retired the Basic Display API (2024-12-04). Impact: the connection token does not match the API the adapter calls. Proposed fix: move the connection to the Instagram Graph API login (Facebook Login for Business) with the set `instagram_basic`, `instagram_content_publish` and `pages_show_list` the adapter needs; a unit of its own, with tests against a recorded flow. It waits its turn in this queue, like the rest of §5.11.
> - [ ] **DEF-56** — TikTok: direct publishing cannot be authorized. `TikTokApiClient` publishes through `/post/publish/video/init/`, which TikTok conditions on the `video.publish` scope; only the shared metadata (`packages/shared/src/providers/providerConfig.ts:212`) and the package's own authentication service (`packages/providers/tiktok/src/apiClient.ts:112`) list it, while the connection configuration (`apps/api/src/auth/providerOAuthConfigs.ts:405`) requests only `user.info.basic` and `video.upload`, and `packages/providers/tiktok/src/TikTokAdapter.ts:71` does not declare it either. Impact: a connected TikTok channel cannot complete a direct publish. Proposed fix: request `video.publish` where the product publishes directly, align the adapter and test as in DEF-54; a unit of its own. It waits its turn in this queue, like the rest of §5.11.
> - [ ] **DEF-57** — LinkedIn: publishing as an organization cannot work. `packages/providers/linkedin/src/LinkedInAdapter.ts:64` and `packages/shared/src/providers/providerConfig.ts:241` declare `w_organization_social`, and `LinkedInApiClient` acts as the organization when the credentials carry an `organizationUrn`, but the connection configuration (`apps/api/src/auth/providerOAuthConfigs.ts:467`) requests only `openid`, `profile` and `w_member_social`. Impact: the token does not authorize writing on behalf of an organization. Proposed fix: request the scope, or make publishing as an organization an explicit option with its own consent step, and test as in DEF-54; the Product Owner decides between the two. It waits its turn in this queue, like the rest of §5.11.
> - [ ] **DEF-58** — LinkedIn: the connection step reads the email without the `email` scope. `apps/api/src/auth/providerOAuthConfigs.ts:511` reads `userInfo.email` while the requested scopes are `openid`, `profile` and `w_member_social` (`:467`). Impact: the stored username stays empty on LinkedIn channels. Proposed fix: request `email` or stop reading it, with a test over the fixture of the connection step; a unit of its own. It waits its turn in this queue, like the rest of §5.11.
> - [ ] **DEF-59** — Pinterest: the connection step calls `/v5/user_account` without the `user_accounts:read` scope. `packages/providers/pinterest/src/PinterestAdapter.ts:67` declares the scope; the connection step (`apps/api/src/auth/providerOAuthConfigs.ts:553`) and `PinterestApiClient.getUserAccount` call `/v5/user_account`, which requires it, but the connection configuration (`:526`) requests only the four board and pin scopes. Impact: the account read can be rejected after connecting. Proposed fix: request the scope and test as in DEF-54; a unit of its own. It waits its turn in this queue, like the rest of §5.11.
> - [ ] **DEF-60** — Threads has adapter scopes but no connection flow. `packages/providers/threads/src/ThreadsAdapter.ts:67-72` declares four scopes, and `apps/api/src/auth/providerOAuthConfigs.ts:646` registers Threads with `createUnimplementedProvider`. Impact: no Threads account can be connected, while the adapter and the product's provider list present it as available. Proposed fix: implement the Threads OAuth flow or withdraw Threads from the connectable providers until it exists; it is a product decision of the Product Owner before any code. It waits its turn in this queue, like the rest of §5.11.
> - [ ] **DEF-61** — the OAuth scopes are declared in five places that do not match: the connection configuration, the adapters' `requiredScopes`, the shared `PROVIDER_CONFIGS` (which feed `apps/api/src/providers/providerRegistry.ts` and the client's `useProviders`), the client's `getDefaultScopes` (`apps/client/lib/utils/providerMapper.ts:112`, which holds names that are not scopes, such as `read`, `write` or `r_liteprofile`, and which are shown to the user) and TikTok's in `packages/providers/tiktok/src/apiClient.ts:112`; in addition, `HubSpotAdapter` and `SalesforceAdapter` have a `getAuthorizationUrl` of their own, a second CRM authorization URL, where HubSpot adds `crm.objects.companies.read` and the routes (`apps/api/src/crm/crmRoutes.ts:251`) never call it. Also, `packages/providers/youtube/src/YouTubeAdapter.ts:70` writes short names (`youtube.upload`, `youtube.readonly`) that are not Google scope strings, while the connection configuration uses the full URLs, and the shared metadata do not include `tiktok:user.info.basic`. Impact: each declaration can diverge without anything detecting it, and the user sees permissions the product does not request. Proposed fix: a single source per provider (the adapter's declaration, consumed by the connection configuration and by the shared metadata), the client's mapper reading it, and the inventory's Mismatches section as the regression oracle; first a design decision and then its unit. It waits its turn in this queue, like the rest of §5.11.
> - [ ] **DEF-62** — more access is requested than is used: `pinterest:boards:write` (`createBoard` has no caller) and `hubspot:crm.objects.contacts.write` (`HubSpotAdapter` never writes a contact) are requested without use, and the inventory classifies them as `unused`. Impact: data minimization (GDPR, art. 5(1)(c)) and user trust. Proposed fix: withdraw the scopes or deliver the features that need them; the Product Owner decides scope by scope. It waits its turn in this queue, like the rest of §5.11.

> **Added on 2026-10-08 by the handlebars CVE floor (#492)** — DEF-66, found while measuring the reach of GHSA-8r5x-fm3f-whwj, whose fix in 4.7.10 warns never to compile a stored AST where users can write:
>
> - [ ] **DEF-66** — two paths pass to `Handlebars.compile` the content of a `Json` column converted with `as string`: `ServerTemplateEngine.compileComponent` with `TemplateComponent.content` and `compileWithABTest` with `Template.variants`. Today they are dormant: nothing in `apps` or in `packages` writes `TemplateComponent`, no route calls `compileTemplateWithComponents`, and the template creation and edit handlers do not write `variants`. If either one is activated, a stored object would reach `compile` as an AST. Proposed fix: validate that the content is a string before compiling (or type the column as text) and cover it with a test that rejects an object.

> - [ ] **DEF-71** — polling a saga's status requests faster than its limiter bucket refills. `GET /sagas/:sagaId` falls in the STANDARD preset (100 per minute per IP and resource: the `defaultConfig: RateLimitConfigs.STANDARD` that `apps/api/src/index.ts:518` passes to the preHandler), with one bucket per saga since DEF-68. `runSagaAndAwaitTerminal` (`apps/client/lib/api/clients/sagaClient.ts:178-199`), which the pages of the post and of its preview, `useSchedulePostViaSaga` and `lib/api/hooks.ts` use, polls every 200 ms (300 per minute) for up to 60 s: the bucket, full at the start, empties at 30 s, and from then on two of every three polls receive 429, which the loop discards with `continue` without reading `Retry-After`. `useSagaStatus` (`apps/client/lib/hooks/useSagaStatus.ts:26`, 1 s) stays within the limit. Two tests poll the route over HTTP faster than the bucket: `sagaCustomerFlow.live.test.ts:97` (200 ms) and `production.live.test.ts:97` (250 ms). The full-tier run of 2026-10-09 with the limiter on recorded 289 429 responses on `GET /sagas/:sagaId` and passed anyway, because the polls retry. Proposed fix: the polling honors `Retry-After` and does not request faster than the bucket refills (600 ms with STANDARD), with a test that pins it. It waits its turn in this queue, like the rest of §5.11.

It touches the legal register of §5.10. Proposed fix: a CI step that fails on a license outside a reviewed list, with the red path proven; the list itself is a decision of the Product Owner. > - [ ] **DEF-77** — the scheduled `OWASP ZAP DAST` job (`.github/workflows/security-testing.yml:168-177`) ends red without a single failing finding. The scheduled run of 2026-10-09 gave 0 FAIL, 68 PASS and 2 WARN («Non-Storable Content» [10049] ×3 and «Sec-Fetch-Dest Header is Missing» [90005] ×11), and with `fail_action: true` the action also fails on WARN. There is no rules file that triages those alerts, and the action tries to open an issue without the `issues: write` permission («Resource not accessible by integration»). `docs/reports/UNUSED_CODE_INVENTORY.md:147` describes it with `fail_action: false`, which no longer holds. A permanent red hides the day a real FAIL appears. Proposed fix: a versioned rules file that triages each alert with its reason, the permission the issue step needs or removing that step, and the red path proven with a planted FAIL; it touches a workflow, so it needs a `sensitive-edit` token. It waits its turn in this queue, like the rest of §5.11. **Maintainer decision (2026-10-18):** replace `zaproxy/action-baseline@v0.15.0` (`security-testing.yml:166`) with ZAP's API scan (`zaproxy/action-api-scan`), fed with the app's OpenAPI specification, the one the versioned route table projects (`apps/api/scripts/dump-openapi-spec.ts`), so that it walks the real routes; a versioned rules file that triages each alert, with its reason, after looking at it; remove the step that opens the issue, because the red job is the signal; and the red path proven with a planted FAIL. A real finding of the API scan is fixed, not suppressed. Measured: the scheduled runs of 2026-10-16, 2026-10-17 and 2026-10-18 fail with 0 FAIL and 2 WARN (10049 ×3, 90005 ×10) and with «Resource not accessible by integration» in the issue step, and the baseline scan visits only `/`, `/robots.txt` and `/sitemap.xml`, so the 68 PASS of the 2026-10-09 run measure almost nothing. In its turn, with a `sensitive-edit` token. With the document's relative server (DEF-143), an API scan that reads the document from a file needs `-O http://localhost:3000` to set the host.

> **Added on 2026-10-10 by the fix of DEF-72** — DEF-79 and DEF-80, found while measuring that fix; they are registered and wait their turn, with no fix in that unit:

> - [ ] **DEF-79** — cancelling or rescheduling a scheduled post neither stops nor moves its publish job. The two Admin routes, `POST /admin/posts/:id/cancel` and `POST /admin/posts/:id/reschedule` (`apps/api/src/admin/schedulingRoutes.ts:33,42`), build their handler with only the Prisma client (`:21`) and never call the queue. `SchedulingPostHandlers.ts:211-299` moves the post to `DRAFT`, clears `scheduledAt` and marks as `ERR` its queued or in-progress publish records; `:305-402` moves it to `SCHEDULED` with the new time and, with `updateChannels`, writes it into those records. The job is in BullMQ already since the saga's pivot, with `runAt` equal to the original time (`packages/shared/src/saga.ts:798-810`, `apps/api/src/saga/SagaIntegration.ts:296-303`), and the worker does not read the post's status before publishing: `handleJob` (`apps/workers/src/publishHandler.ts:721-774`) only skips a `dedupeKey` whose record is already `OK` and a deleted post, project or account chain (`SOFT_DELETED`), and `getPostById` (`packages/adapters/db-prisma/src/PostRepository.ts:25-36`) looks up by `id` without looking at the status. So a job already queued publishes at its original time after cancelling or rescheduling. A customer has neither route, and `DeletePostUseCase` (`packages/core/posts/src/DeletePostUseCase.ts:112`) does not allow deleting a scheduled post. Verified statically, not run; found while rewriting the API reference for DEF-72, which already documents this limitation. Proposed fix: cancelling removes and rescheduling re-enqueues the post's jobs through the queue port, and the worker discards a job whose post is no longer scheduled or publishing, with an integration test that cancels a scheduled post and checks that, when its time comes, there is no call to the provider. It waits its turn in this queue, like the rest of §5.11.

> - [ ] **DEF-80** — the API reference documents routes that the generated route table (`packages/shared/src/api-generated/types.gen.ts`) does not have, outside the publishing surface DEF-72 fixed. In [`docs/api/README.md`](https://github.com/edwardricardo/omni-post/blob/fb399b4897b6e2229dac2a53ab3a488a46620d49/docs/api/README.md): `GET /health/full` (`:34`), `PUT /posts/{postId}` (`:185`), `GET /analytics` with filters (`:312`), `POST /analytics/fetch` (`:339`) and `PUT /admin/accounts/{accountId}/suspend` (`:400`). In `docs/architecture/API.md`: those five (`:49`, `:272`, `:456`, `:483` and `:592`) and also `POST /auth/rbac/roles` (`:186`), `POST /auth/rbac/assign` (`:199`), `POST /posts/{postId}/media` (`:287`), `POST /media/sign` (`:303`), `GET /audit/logs` (`:511`), `POST /billing/trial/start` (`:626`), `POST /billing/subscription/convert` (`:640`), `GET /providers/health` (`:655`), `GET /providers/x/capabilities` (`:662`) and `POST /providers/x/validate` (`:669`), plus two WebSockets with no registered route, `ws://localhost:3000/analytics/realtime` (`:497` and `:761`) and `ws://localhost:3000/admin/dashboard/live` (`:767`). Two of them have the wrong method: the table registers `PATCH /posts/{id}` and `POST /admin/accounts/{accountId}/suspend`. The two response-format sections (`README.md:11-19` and `API.md:24-32`) claim `{ ok: true, value: T }`, but the handlers over `BaseRouteHandler` answer `{ ok: true, data }` and `{ ok: false, error, details }` (`apps/api/src/lib/route-handler/BaseRouteHandler.ts:263-286`) and the saga routes `{ success: true, data }` (`apps/api/src/saga/SagaIntegration.ts:480,534`). In addition, `openspec/changes/auth-rate-limit-integrity/design.md:291` and `:502` cite `docs/architecture/API.md:669-672` and `docs/api/README.md:383-385` for the rate-limit headers, which after DEF-72 are at `API.md:739-742` and `README.md:434-436`. Measured on 2026-10-10 while closing DEF-72, against the 500 method+route pairs of the table. `docs/api/README.md` was deleted from the repository on 2026-10-17 (git history keeps it) with the rest of the hand-copied API pages, so its half of this row was closed by deletion and what stays open is `docs/architecture/API.md`. Proposed fix: rewrite each section of `API.md` against the generated table and the code of its route, or remove the one that documents something that does not exist; fix its response-format section, and make `design.md` cite the headers of `API.md` by their content and not by line number (since 2026-10-17, its citation of `docs/api/README.md` is a permalink to the version of that page it measured). It waits its turn in this queue, like the rest of §5.11.

The `predev` of `apps/admin/package.json:9` and of `apps/client/package.json:9` (`pnpm --filter @shared/types... build`) only builds `@shared/types` and its dependencies, and the `dev` task of `turbo.json:54` only depends on `@shared/types#build`; the packages the portal imports through their `dist`, such as `@packages/i18n` (its `exports` points to `./dist/index.js`), stay unbuilt. Measured on 2026-10-10 in Admin: `GET /en/login` answered 500 with `Module not found: Can't resolve '@packages/i18n'` from `apps/admin/i18n/request.ts:9`; the client imports the same package in `apps/client/i18n/request.ts:9`. After `pnpm exec turbo run build --filter='@apps/admin^...' --filter='@apps/client^...'` (19 tasks, 11 s), both portals answer 200. The main checkout does not show it because it keeps those `dist` from earlier builds. It was measured with Webpack; the cause, a `dist` that does not exist, does not depend on the bundler. Proposed fix: the `predev` and the `dev` task of `turbo.json` build every workspace dependency of the portal (`^build`), not only `@shared/types`. It waits its turn in this queue, like the rest of §5.11.

Next 16 always sets the development `distDir` to `.next/dev` (`next/dist/server/config.js:1248-1250`, in 16.3.8), so `next dev` rewrites the file to import `.next/dev/types`, while `next build` and `next typegen` write `.next/types`: every development run leaves a change in the tree. The guide the package itself ships asks to ignore it and take it out of Git (`next/dist/docs/01-app/03-api-reference/05-config/02-typescript.md:120`). Untracking it requires running `next typegen` before the typecheck scripts (`typecheck` in `apps/admin/package.json:16` and `apps/client/package.json:21`, and `type-check` in `apps/client/package.json:31`), which today read the tracked file through their `tsconfig.json` (`apps/admin/tsconfig.json:26`, `apps/client/tsconfig.json:35`). Measured on 2026-10-10: every `next dev` of the branch `workstream/dev-turbopack` left its portal's `next-env.d.ts` modified, and it had to be restored by hand. Proposed next step: untrack both files and run `next typegen` before the typecheck, in a unit of its own. It waits its turn in this queue, like the rest of §5.11.

It is the only tracked `.md` under `apps/`, `packages/` and `infra/` (`git ls-files`). Its three lines explain that the directory holds the files Next serves as is from `/` and that it must exist even when empty, because `apps/admin/Dockerfile:96` copies it into the image and a missing directory breaks that stage; as it is the only file in the directory, the README itself is what keeps it in Git. Nothing detects a new misplaced `.md` under `apps/` or `packages/`: no fitness and no gate of `scripts/` covers it, and the pre-edit hooks of `.claude/hooks-py` skip `.md` files (`pre_edit_tripwire_blocker.py:262`, `pre_edit_planmode_guard.py:62`) and never see the files a tool writes, such as the `AGENTS.md` and `CLAUDE.md` of `next dev`. Proposed fix: move its explanation to `docs/` and keep the directory with a file that is not `.md`, or make the `Dockerfile` not depend on it, because deleting the README outright breaks that copy; then, a gate with its red path proven. It waits its turn in this queue, like the rest of §5.11.

> - [ ] **DEF-90** — the copyleft license check was the only useful value of its own in the bundle analyzer DEF-84 deleted, and it only ran by hand, with `pnpm quality:bundle`: it read `pnpm licenses list --json` and flagged every installed package whose license expression contained `GPL-2.0`, `GPL-3.0` or `AGPL-3.0` (the substring match also flagged `LGPL-2.0` and `LGPL-3.0`, but not `LGPL-2.1`), without failing anything and returning an empty list when pnpm could not list the licenses. No gate covers that check: `pnpm quality:security` lists the licenses without judging them. It needs a real gate, one that reads `pnpm licenses list --json` and fails on a copyleft expression, with its red path proven, probably beside the legal dependency inventory (`docs/legal/inventories/subprocessors.generated.md`, fitness #45) or inside `pnpm check:legal`. Where it lives and which expressions count as copyleft is a decision of its own unit. It waits its turn in this queue, like the rest of §5.11.

> - [ ] **DEF-91** — `setupTest` of `apps/api/tests/setup.ts` logs «DB/Redis connections verified» without inspecting the `Result` of `queue.health()` or that of `listLogs({ limit: 1 })`: both are awaited with `Promise.all` and the value is discarded (`_healthResults`), so a database or Redis error returned as `err` passes the setup, and only a rejection or the time limit (`TEST_CONFIG.dbTimeout`) make it fail. In addition, when it fails, the `catch` drops both adapters (`globalRepo = null`, `globalQueue = null`) without closing them, and the next call creates others. Proposed fix: narrow both results and make the setup fail on an `err`, closing what was opened. It waits its turn in this queue, like the rest of §5.11.

> **Added on 2026-10-18** — DEF-105 to DEF-109, found during the fixed-wait sweep of N-CI-7 (#641 to #646) and while ordering its PR chain; measured statically on `main` at `30d9c8cb`. They are registered without a fix and wait their turn, like the rest of §5.11:
>
> - [ ] **DEF-105** — two tests of the AI orchestrator assert a behavior that does not exist. The orchestrator has no per-call time limit: `AITaskConfig.timeout` (`apps/api/src/ai/types.ts:48`) is set to 30000 (`apps/api/src/ai/orchestrator.ts:128`, `:147` and `:165`) and nothing reads it; and `getOptimalProvider` (`orchestrator.ts:191-209`) tries the providers in the fixed order of `providerStrengths`, without looking at latency. «should handle provider timeout gracefully» (`apps/api/tests/unit/aiOrchestrator.content.test.ts:226`) passes because the slow provider ends up answering at 2 s, not because another one replaces it, and «should prefer faster providers over time» (`apps/api/tests/unit/aiOrchestrator.providers.test.ts:200`) passes because `openai` goes first in that fixed order. Proposed fix: decide whether the orchestrator must have a per-call time limit and an order by latency, and implement them with their tests, or rewrite the two tests so they assert the behavior that exists; the decision is a product one. Found in U4b (#644). **Product Owner decision (2026-10-18):** rewrite the two tests so they assert the behavior that exists (the provider that fails or exceeds its time limit is skipped and the next one answers; the choice follows the fixed order of each task), delete `AITaskConfig.timeout` and its three assignments, and build no order by latency; in Phase 2 of the Testing Refoundation or in its turn.

> - [ ] **DEF-107** — DEF-94 counts ten actionlint findings in `fitness.yml`; on `30d9c8cb`, `actionlint -shellcheck "$(which shellcheck)" .github/workflows/*.yml` (actionlint 1.7.12) reports 81 in eight workflows, exiting with 1: `dependency-updates.yml` 32, `cleanup.yml` 17, `audit.yml` 14, `fitness.yml` 10, `ci.yml` 4, `performance.yml` 2, `chaos.yml` 1 and `security-testing.yml` 1; by code, SC2086 54, SC2016 7, SC2002 7, SC2129 6, SC2155 3 (`ci.yml:435`, `:528` and `chaos.yml:112`), SC2044 2, SC2254 1 and SC2015 1. No step of CI, of the battery or of the hooks runs actionlint. Proposed fix: that of DEF-94 over the 81, each workflow as a modified gate with its red path proven, and then actionlint with shellcheck as a battery step and as a CI job. Found while reviewing the N-CI-7 overnight run. **Maintainer decision (2026-10-18):** DEF-107 absorbs DEF-94; fix the 81 findings, each workflow as a modified gate with its red path proven, and then add `actionlint -shellcheck` as a battery step and as a CI job. In its turn; it touches workflows, so it needs a `sensitive-edit` token.
> - [ ] **DEF-108** — the database circuit breaker of the shared adapter counts a domain error as an outage. `createDatabaseCircuitBreaker` (`packages/adapters/db-prisma/src/resilience.ts:57-68`) creates an opossum 10.0.0 breaker without `errorFilter` (the default counts every rejection) and without `volumeThreshold` (0 by default), and `createPrismaRepoAdapter` (`packages/adapters/db-prisma/src/index.ts:102-122`) opens the writes breaker at 40 % failures for 60 s and the transactions breaker at 30 % for 90 s. With `volumeThreshold` 0, a single error in a window with no other calls is enough: a `createPost` for a project that does not exist (`PostRepository.ts:146-151` throws inside the transaction) or a duplicate email (P2002) in `createAccount` (`AccountRepository.ts:44`) would open the breaker for every tenant. Latent: a static search found no production caller of the adapter's `createPost` or `createAccount`. Proposed fix: an `errorFilter` that leaves domain and constraint errors out, and a measured `volumeThreshold`; what counts as an outage is a decision of the Maintainer. Found while ordering the N-CI-7 chain. **Maintainer decision (2026-10-18), together with DEF-109:** demolish instead of fixing. Measured on `28807cef`: the three breakers protect only `createAccount`, `getAccountById` and `createPost`, none with a production caller, and `createPost` duplicates the path of `CreatePostUseCase`. The three methods are deleted from the adapter and from `RepoPort` (`packages/ports/src/RepoPort.ts:138`, `:142` and `:222`) with their tests, the three breakers with their metrics wiring, and `perf:baseline` moves to seeding through the seed client or the real use cases.
> - [ ] **DEF-109** — `createPost` of the same adapter (`packages/adapters/db-prisma/src/PostRepository.ts:142-145`) looks up the project with `tx.project.findFirst({ where: { id: input.projectId, deletedAt: null } })` in a transaction without a tenant, on purpose; if `input.projectId` arrives `undefined` at run time, Prisma takes `id: undefined` as the absence of a filter and returns any live project of any account, so the «project does not exist» guard does not fire and the creation fails later with a Prisma validation error (`Post.projectId` is required) instead of the guard's message. The type `CreatePostInput.projectId: string` (`packages/ports/src/RepoPort.ts:59`) excludes it at compile time, not at run time. Latent, like DEF-108. Proposed fix: reject an empty `projectId` before the query. Found while ordering the N-CI-7 chain. **Maintainer decision (2026-10-18):** it is resolved with the demolition of DEF-108.
>
> **Added on 2026-10-18** — DEF-110 to DEF-112: DEF-110 and DEF-111, found by unit K5c of N-CI-7 (#651) while making no saga run outlive its test; DEF-112, found by unit O-F of N-CI-7 (#655) while making the bulk-scheduling reconciliation suite independent of order. They are registered without a fix and wait their turn, like the rest of §5.11:
>
> - [ ] **DEF-110** — `SagaManagerLifecycle.shutdown()` (`apps/api/src/saga/SagaManagerLifecycle.ts:1179-1228`) neither waits for nor cancels the detached runs. `startSaga` dispatches its run with `executeSagaAsync` (`:894`), and `shutdown()` empties `activeInstances` and `definitions` (`:1222-1225`) without waiting for the dispatched runs. Measured: a run dispatched before `shutdown()` runs afterwards and logs at error level «Saga definition not found» (`apps/api/src/saga/SagaManagerExecution.ts:492`); the saga unit run logged 17 of those lines before #651. Read, not measured: a run that is already inside a step keeps its definition, and its later writes can overwrite the hand-over to `PENDING` that `shutdown()` does, because the compare-and-swap of `SagaManagerExecution.ts:1576-1580` rejects only the terminal states. Proposed fix: `shutdown()` waits for or cancels each run in progress before handing over its sagas; which of the two is a decision of the Maintainer. Found in K5c (#651). **Maintainer decision (2026-10-18):** wait, do not cancel: `shutdown()` stops accepting new runs, waits for the ones in progress with a cap shorter than the platform's shutdown deadline and then hands over the ones left; in addition, the persistence compare-and-swap rejects a write that advances over a row already handed over (it requires `RUNNING`), so that a run that outlives the cap does not overwrite the `PENDING`. Cancelling requires every step to honor the abort and does not undo an external call already sent: it only loses its record. In its turn, together with DEF-111.
> - [ ] **DEF-111** — `shutdown()` empties the `activeInstances` map (`SagaManagerLifecycle.ts:1222`) without going through `stopTracking` (`:1835-1841`), the only one that decrements `metrics.activeInstances` (`:1839`), so the metric keeps its last value after the shutdown. Measured: it stays at 2 after closing the manager with two parked sagas. Proposed fix: decrement each instance when emptying the map, with a test that pins the metric at zero after the shutdown. Found in K5c (#651). **Maintainer decision (2026-10-18):** it is fixed in the same unit as DEF-110.
> - [ ] **DEF-112** — the bulk-scheduling reconciliation always re-enqueues the same rows of the dead-letter queue and never reaches the others. `sweep()` (`apps/api/src/bulk-scheduling/BulkScheduleReconciliationService.ts:95-102`) reads from `OutboxDeadLetter` up to `MAX_PER_TICK = 50` (`:32`) unresolved `BulkScheduleItem` rows, with no `orderBy`, re-enqueues each one (with the deduplication key `bulk-${batchId}-${itemId}`, `:151`) and never writes `resolvedAt`: only the admin routes of `apps/api/src/outbox/outboxAdminRoutes.ts` (`:90` and `:115`) resolve rows, by hand. So the same rows, up to 50, are processed again every 60 s (`:72`) until someone resolves them, and the ones beyond those 50 are never processed again. Read, not measured in production. Proposed fix: decide when a re-enqueued row is resolved and in what order the rows are taken, with a test for more than 50 rows; the decision is a product one, the Product Owner's. Found in O-F (#655). **Product Owner decision (2026-10-18):** mark the row resolved (`resolvedAt`, reason «re-enqueued by the reconciliation») as soon as its re-enqueue succeeds, and take the rows from the oldest to the newest, with a test of more than 50 rows. Measured: the `bulk-schedule` queue configures neither `removeOnComplete` nor `removeOnFail` (`apps/api/src/infrastructure/container/setupServices.ts:678-681`), so today a repeated re-enqueue is a no-op and does not duplicate; the defect is the starvation beyond 50 rows and the trap of turning on `removeOnComplete`. A re-enqueued job that fails again already goes to `bulk-schedule-dead-letter` (`apps/api/src/index.ts:1202-1215`).
>
> **Added on 2026-10-18** — DEF-113 to DEF-116, found by N-AUTH-1 (§1): DEF-113 while planning it, DEF-114 while fixing the ENTERPRISE quota, DEF-115 while making the assertions of its live tier exact and DEF-116 by `pnpm check:duplicates`. Read and measured on `c8c826fe`; they are registered without a fix and wait their turn, like the rest of §5.11:
>
> - [ ] **DEF-113** — the Admin usage panel never loads. `UsageMetricsPanel` (`apps/admin/components/settings/UsageMetricsPanel.tsx:62`, mounted in `apps/admin/app/[locale]/(dashboard)/subscriptions/page.tsx:381`) asks for its data with `useUsageMetrics` (`apps/admin/hooks/api/useUsageMetrics.ts:27`) to `GET /accounts/:accountId/usage` through the Admin proxy, which forwards the Admin token (`apps/admin/app/api/backend/[...path]/route.ts:76`); the route is a customer one (`requireClientAuth`, `apps/api/src/usage/usageRoutes.ts:103`) and `verifyCustomerToken` rejects every token whose `type` is not `customer` (`apps/api/src/auth/customerJwt.ts:96`), so the panel always receives 401. It was already so on `main` at `28807cef`, before N-AUTH-1, and the generated route table has no Admin route for an account's usage (`packages/shared/src/api-generated/types.gen.ts`, only `/accounts/{accountId}/usage`). Read, not measured at run time. Proposed fix: `GET /admin/accounts/:accountId/usage` with `requireAdminAuth`, its permission and `withSystemContext`, and the Admin hook pointed at it. **Product Owner decision (2026-10-18), question 4 of N-AUTH-1:** an item of its own, with no code in N-AUTH-1.
> - [ ] **DEF-114** — outside `createProject`, the `maxProjects = -1` of ENTERPRISE (unlimited: `packages/core/domain/src/entities/Account.ts:56`, and `canCreateProject` at `:354`) is used as a number. `TrialManagementService.buildTrialResponse` (`packages/core/billing/src/TrialManagementService.ts:108` and `:110`) and `dashboardService.mapAccountToSummary` (`apps/api/src/admin/dashboardService.ts:124`, `:153` and `:155`) answer, for a limit of -1, 0 projects remaining and 0 % usage instead of unlimited. `SubscriptionManagementService.validateSubscriptionLimits` (`packages/core/billing/src/SubscriptionManagementService.ts:83-84`, `:93` and `:110`), behind `POST /admin/billing/accounts/:accountId/validate-limits` (`apps/api/src/billing/subscriptionRoutes.ts:96`), would deny the creation of a project and would compute negative team and storage limits (`-1 * 5`, `-1 * 10`), but it reads `AccountSubscription.maxProjects` (`apps/api/src/infrastructure/repositories/PrismaAccountSubscriptionQueryRepository.ts:142-148`), not the account's column, and the subscription use cases that were read set it to 3 or to 3 per provider (`packages/core/billing/src/CreateAccountSubscriptionUseCase.ts:117-164`, `ChangeAccountSubscriptionUseCase.ts:130-219`): latent. Read, not measured at run time. Proposed fix: a single unlimited-limit predicate in the domain, the `-1` of `Account.ts:354`, used by the three places, with a test of each for -1.
> - [ ] **DEF-115** — an existing post that the saga schedules stays in `DRAFT` and can be deleted while its publish jobs wait for their time. With `mode: "schedule"` and a `postId`, `CreatePostStep` does not touch the post (`packages/shared/src/saga.ts:601-615`, `skippedCreation: true`), the pivot enqueues one job per channel, and `WaitForPublishingCompletionStep` and `UpdatePostStatusStep` finish without doing anything in that mode (`saga.ts:860` and `:990`), although the second one's JSDoc says the post stays in `DRAFT`/`SCHEDULED` (`:976-977`). **Measured** in the live tier of N-AUTH-1 (`apps/api/tests/production.live.test.ts`, «should schedule publication» and «should delete post», 179 of 179 green): with the saga in `COMPLETED`, `DELETE /posts/:postId` answers 200, because `DeletePostUseCase` deletes an editable post (`packages/core/posts/src/DeletePostUseCase.ts:112`). The deletion is soft (`packages/adapters/db-prisma/src/post/PrismaPostRepository.ts:132-134`) and the worker discards the job of a deleted post (`apps/workers/src/publishHandler.ts:746-762`), so it does not publish; what fails is the status: the post shows as an editable draft while its jobs wait. It contradicts two records: DEF-79 says that `DeletePostUseCase` does not allow deleting a scheduled post, true only for a post in `SCHEDULED`, and WU-4.3 of the Testing Refoundation plan expects 403 for that deletion (`docs/development/TESTING_REFOUNDATION_PLAN.md:1311`). N-COR-9 already records that the saga skips the `schedule` mode; a new post created in that mode was not measured. Proposed fix: the `schedule` mode leaves the post in `SCHEDULED` through the aggregate, and the live test of the deletion asserts 403 with the domain code; which saga step writes the status is a design decision.
> - [ ] **DEF-116** — the Admin login and the customer login repeat a block: `apps/api/src/auth/authRoutes.ts:41-59` and `apps/api/src/auth/customerAuthRoutes.ts:180-200` (19 lines, 89 tokens; measured with jscpd over the two files, with the thresholds of `.jscpd.json`). It is a baseline clone: `c8c826fe` added inside its span the JSDoc of `login` that fitness #47 requires, and since jscpd's fingerprint covers the raw text, comments included, it went from `d5cf65c1e8562321` to `09c2cc687c1608ad`; the baseline swapped one fingerprint for the other without raising the count. Proposed fix: move what the two handlers share (the validation of the body and its error response) to a common function and delete the baseline entry.

> **Added on 2026-10-26** — DEF-117 to DEF-121, found by the ui-locales unit (`PR uiloc`, `workstream/refound-2-ui-locales`) while measuring the proxies of both portals. DEF-117 to DEF-119 are closed in that same unit; DEF-120 and DEF-121 are registered without a fix and wait their turn:
>

> - [x] **DEF-118** — an open redirect (CWE-601) in Admin's `GET /api/auth/refresh`; done 2026-10-26 by #740 (merge `0c9827f0`).

> **Added on 2026-10-26** — DEF-122 to DEF-128, found by the locale-formatting unit (`PR lfmt`, `workstream/refound-2-locale-formatting`), which brought to the active language the 23 date and number formats that named `"en-US"`. They are registered without a fix and wait their turn:
>
> - [x] **DEF-122** — hand-written English text on portal screens, mixed with localized dates for a Spanish-language user; done 2026-10-26 by #742 (merge `3eafca50`).
> - [ ] **DEF-123** — money is left half localized: `analytics/page.tsx:29` uses `Intl.NumberFormat` with `currency` ("1.234.567 US$" in Spanish) and `:384`, `subscriptions/page.tsx:424,428,467` prepend "$" to a localized figure ("$7.654.321"); `AccountBillingPanel.tsx:180-318` builds prices with `toFixed(2)`; percentages and K/M/B suffixes by hand in `analytics/page.tsx:32-35,168-315` and `subscriptions/page.tsx:438`. It asks to decide where the currency comes from (today a fixed USD) and to accept that English edge cases change. **Decided on 2026-10-28 (Product Owner): accepted** the recommendation — a single source of next-intl formats (`formats.number` in `packages/i18n/src/createRequestConfig.ts`, read with `useFormatter`) for every amount, percentage and suffix, with USD fixed in a single place and no "$" written in the catalogs. Taking the currency from the data (today only `Invoice.currency` stores it, `infra/prisma/schema.prisma:3122`) is a migration that is done only if the product charges in another currency.
> - [ ] **DEF-124** — `cronstrue` describes the crons only in English (`apps/client/components/scheduling/RecurringPostCard.tsx:28`, `apps/client/components/analytics/ScheduledReportsList.tsx:24`); `cronstrue/i18n` brings every language, a bundle-size decision. **Decided on 2026-10-28 (Product Owner):** "Is it something the user can set? If so, it must be in every available language." It is: `RecurrenceSelector.tsx` offers a free cron input, `CreateReportForm.tsx` a text field, and the API only validates the length. One cronstrue locale is imported for each language of `routing.locales` (about 1.4 KB gzip each; `cronstrue/i18n`, with its 38 languages, weighs about 30 KB gzip), with a test that fails if a language of the routing does not have its own.
> - [ ] **DEF-125** — `apps/client/components/scheduling/SchedulingDashboardCalendar.tsx:122` builds an aria-label with `toDateString()`: a Spanish screen reader says "Seleccionar Thu Oct 29 2026". Localizing it changes the accessible name in English.

A product decision. **Decided on 2026-10-28 (Product Owner):** "It depends on how it is triggered: if it is from the schedule, the last zone used is used; if the user has an open session, the session's current zone is used." The interpretation is confirmed with the Product Owner when its unit arrives.

A tests typecheck per portal is needed, like the ratchet of `apps/api`.

Decide whether the API sends codes (plan type, reason code) that the portal names with its catalog.

Decide how an error is localized: a code the portal translates.

The keys portal-literals added have them. One pass over `apps/admin/messages/es.json`.

And the Admin analytics panel labels metrics it does not measure (`apps/admin/hooks/api/useAnalytics.ts:124-137`): «Security Score» is the engagement rate, «Data Processed» the number of posts with a «B» glued on, «System Uptime» a fixed 100, «Error Rate» 100 minus the publish success rate and «Feature Adoption» the channels per account.

DEF-135 to DEF-137 are closed in that same unit; DEF-138 to DEF-141 are registered without a fix and wait their turn:

> - [ ] **DEF-139** — the glossary and the style guide (and, because of that, the casts of `apps/api/tests/eval/localizedGeneration.eval.test.ts:73-74`) type their language as `string` in the domain and in their adapters (`packages/core/domain/src/repositories/GlossaryRepository.ts`, `StyleGuideRuleRepository.ts`, `SemanticRetrievalPort.ts`, the use cases of `packages/core/glossary` and `packages/core/style-guide`, `PrismaGlossaryRepository.ts`, `PrismaStyleGuideRuleRepository.ts`). The AI routes already accept only `es` and `en`, so no other value gets in today, but the type does not say so. Fix: `ContentLocale` end to end and `contentLocaleFromStorage` on reading.
> - [ ] **DEF-140** — the default language of a project is `es` in the database (`infra/prisma/schema.prisma:643`, `@default("es")`) and `en` in the API (`apps/api/src/projects/projectRoutes.ts`, `Project.create`, `Content.create` and the post creation command); the adapter of `@adapters/db-prisma` fell back to `es` until content-languages. Which language is the default is a product decision. **Decided on 2026-10-28 (Product Owner):** the default language of a project is `en`, in the database and in the API.
> - [ ] **DEF-141** — `AiClient.generateContent` (`apps/client/lib/api/clients/aiClient.ts`) sends `{ prompt, type, tone, length, language }` to `POST /ai/generate`, whose schema requires `messages` (`apps/api/src/ai/routes.ts:34`): every call would receive 400 and the API ignores `language`. No component calls it. Decide whether it is deleted or wired. **Decided on 2026-10-28 (Product Owner):** it is not deleted: "it is a feature to wire/develop; it is one of the application's flagship features." It is wired against the schema of `POST /ai/generate` as a unit of its own.

> **Added on 2026-10-26** — DEF-142 to DEF-145, found while deciding the dependency ledger (`@fastify/swagger` and `@scalar/fastify-api-reference`, 2026-10-25) and while reading the Scalar plugin in the api-docs unit (`PR apidoc`, `workstream/refound-2-api-docs`). DEF-142 is closed in that unit; DEF-143 and DEF-144 are closed in the scalar-out unit (`PR scalar`, `workstream/refound-2-scalar-out`), which registers DEF-145 without a fix:
>
> - [x] **DEF-142** — the API served its reference and its OpenAPI document in every deployment; done 2026-10-26 by #747 (merge `06cdb655`).
> - [x] **DEF-143** — the OpenAPI document declared `http://localhost:3000` as its only server; done 2026-10-27 by #749 (merge `dbe8fefa`).
> - [x] **DEF-144** — the reference page never rendered, its inline script blocked by the CSP; done 2026-10-27 by #749 (merge `dbe8fefa`).
> - [ ] **DEF-145** — no production code uses the `SecurityConfigs.development` and `SecurityConfigs.production` presets of `apps/api/src/security/securityHeaders.ts`: `createApp()` builds its own configuration inline (`apps/api/src/index.ts:396-411`), and only `tests/unit/securityHeaders.test.ts` and `securityHeaders.mutations.test.ts` read them. Two definitions of the CSP, one of them dead and tested as if it were in force, with values that differ (the development preset turns the CSP off). Decide: `createApp()` reads the preset of its environment, or the presets leave with their tests. **Decided on 2026-10-28 (Maintainer): accepted** the recommendation — the presets and their two tests are deleted, since they pin values of dead code: the development preset sets `["*"]`, which in `@fastify/cors` 11.1.0 lets no browser origin through, and the production one falls back to `https://app.yourapp.com`. The real CORS stays as DEF-146.
>
> **Added on 2026-10-28** — DEF-146 to DEF-150 and a requirement, found while preparing the decisions of DEF-120 to DEF-145 and while reviewing the local observability. They are registered without a fix and wait their turn:
>
> - [ ] **DEF-146** — `createApp()` fixes six `localhost` CORS origins in every environment, production included (`apps/api/src/index.ts:417-422`). The origins have to come from typed variables per environment, with no example domain as fallback (decided with DEF-145).
> - [ ] **DEF-147** — to verify: the client gate looks only at the `customer-session` access cookie (`apps/client/proxy.ts:44`), which lasts 15 minutes (`apps/client/lib/auth/sessionCookie.ts:29`), and not at the refresh one, which lasts 7 or 30 days (`:32`, `:35`). If nothing renews it before it expires, every session idle for more than 15 minutes goes back to the login.
> - [ ] **DEF-148** — Grafana links the Prometheus exemplars to the `jaeger` datasource (`grafana/provisioning/datasources/prometheus.yml:14-16`), but no provisioning file defines it: the jump from a metric to its trace does not work.
> - [ ] **DEF-149** — Jaeger keeps the traces in memory (`jaeger/config.yaml:20`), so they are lost every time it restarts; the requirement below asks not to lose the history.
> - [ ] **DEF-150** — to measure: with `TRACING_ENABLED=true` the API starts the OpenTelemetry SDK (`apps/api/src/index.ts:15-20`) before Sentry (`:469`, `:476`) and does not pass `skipOpenTelemetrySetup`. According to Sentry 10's code, its trace provider does not stay as the global one and its API spans degrade; it is an inference from reading, not measured. The requirement below demands that the two coexist.
> - [ ] **[REQUIREMENT] OpenTelemetry in every application: one error monitor, Sentry or GlitchTip, and an OTLP backend at the same time** (Maintainer, 2026-10-28): "It has to be added to the portals; every application must use it. I don't think, in fact, both should be on at the same time; they should be mutually exclusive, but without deleting the previous history when they are switched." Today the API and the workers export traces over OTLP with `TRACING_ENABLED`; the two portals have no OpenTelemetry. Grafana 11.2.2, Prometheus v2.48.1 and Jaeger exist only in `docker-compose.yml`, and the deployment checklist names none of them. Clarified that same day: "When I referred to mutual exclusion I meant Sentry or GlitchTip, but you can send to either of these 2 and still be able to send to an otpl backend. And yes, everything goes to both sides." So: the four applications (API, workers and the two portals) are instrumented once with OpenTelemetry; each deployment chooses at most one between Sentry and GlitchTip, which speak the same protocol, and can add, at the same time and independently, an OTLP backend; errors and traces go to both destinations, and switching from Sentry to GlitchTip, or the other way round, does not delete the history each one keeps. Making them coexist requires resolving DEF-150: Sentry has to be mounted on the application's OpenTelemetry SDK instead of registering its own.
>
> **Added on 2026-10-28** — DEF-151, registered by the artifact-language workstream (§5.17, decision D6). Recorded without a fix; it waits its turn:
>
> - [ ] **DEF-151** — notifications persist pre-rendered text in one fixed language and ignore the recipient's locale. A `Notification` stores a rendered `title` and `body` (`packages/core/domain/src/entities/Notification.ts:71-79`), and the client shows them as they are (`apps/client/lib/api/clients/notificationsClient.ts:19-20`). Each producer hardcodes one language: `packages/core/notifications/src/handlers/NotificationEventHandlers.ts` writes English (`:60`, `:85`, `:112` and the rest of the file), and `packages/core/inbox/src/handlers/InboxEventHandlers.ts:46-47,76-77` writes Spanish. The recipient's locale exists (`infra/prisma/schema.prisma:28` and `:202`, default `"en"`) and nothing reads it. The fix is a message key with parameters, rendered in the recipient's locale by the portal's i18n. The application is pre-production, so no recipient receives these messages yet. Until the fix, the artifact-language gate allowlists `InboxEventHandlers.ts` with this DEF as its reason, and its strings are not translated: changing user-visible text is this defect's own decision.

### 5.12 Rutas → casos de uso y raíz de composición (ADR-0033) — en cola tras N-TEST-1

> **ℹ️ FICHA [RUTAS-RAÍZ] — decidido y documentado (2026-10-05); nada implementado. En cola: no arranca hasta cerrar N-TEST-1 y el registro legal (§5.10), que va primero; su lugar respecto de §5.9 y §5.11 se fija en ese momento.**
> El ítem (v) de la Fase 0 dejó el gate de arquitectura leyendo el grafo de imports completo (ADR-0032) y verificó contra seis fuentes primarias los 509 bordes que la política anterior rechazaba: el canon permite 460, 43 son violaciones reales y 6 pedían una decisión. Edward decidió el 2026-10-05 que los módulos de ruta reciban sus dependencias como parámetros desde la única raíz de composición, en lugar de resolverlas del contenedor (ADR-0033). El ítem (v) entregó el 2026-10-05 el gate (PR v, #408), la línea base que sólo se achica (PR v-b, #411), el `LoggerPort` del core (PR v-d1, #413 y PR v-d2, #414) y las reparaciones que no requieren diseño (`cacheStatsRoutes`, `queueRoutes` y `healthRoutes`, en PR v-e1, #415 a PR v-e3, #417; ver ARCH-19); esta ficha es el resto: cada módulo de ruta pasa a casos de uso con inyección desde la raíz, área por área. Cada tajada es un PR de hasta unas 400 líneas CODE (una línea movida cuenta dos veces), con sus pruebas y su documentación, y quita sus entradas de la línea base en el mismo cambio; su DoD es la barra §0.2 más el gate de arquitectura en verde, sin entradas obsoletas.
>
> **Decisions:** [ADR-0032](../technical/ADR-0032-architecture-gate-dependency-cruiser.md) (one architecture gate, dependency-cruiser 18.x over the whole resolved graph, with a baseline that only shrinks; `eslint-plugin-boundaries` retires, SMELL-183) and [ADR-0033](../technical/ADR-0033-layer-boundaries-and-composition-root-injection.md) (D-P1 = B, the guards, D-R10, P2b and application-owned ports).
>
> **Estado de desvío, dicho sin rodeos:** hasta que migre su área, 74 de los 78 módulos de ruta siguen resolviendo del contenedor, 23 siguen tocando Prisma, un repositorio o el puerto de email, y cinco construyen objetos de valor (76 y 24 al decidirlo; PR v-e1 y PR v-e3 sacaron del contenedor a `cacheStatsRoutes` y a `healthRoutes`, y esta última dejó de tocar Prisma). Esos bordes sólo se admiten como entradas de la línea base de ADR-0032; un módulo nuevo, o un borde nuevo en uno existente, falla el gate.
>
> **Totales:** los 25 archivos de ruta dimensionados suman ~15 200 líneas CODE (11 400–19 000) en 49 tajadas (55 si se parten las seis marcadas ⚠), de las que `healthRoutes` (~190) entregó el ítem (v) (PR v-e3, #417, `workstream/item-v-cheap-health`, `783aeebe`); los otros 51 módulos de ruta, ~1670 en unas 5 tajadas; la convención de dependencias de rutas en la raíz, ~40; los guards, sin dimensionar. Incertidumbre de ±25 % por archivo, que la verbosidad de las pruebas del repositorio empuja hacia arriba.
>
> **Primera tajada propuesta: TSK-1 (ARCH-15, tareas)**, unas 300 líneas: 260 más la convención de dependencias de rutas en la raíz (~40), que nace con ella. Es el área más chica que fija el patrón completo en una sola tajada:
>
> - un caso de uso de escritura con Unit of Work (`DeleteTaskUseCase`, sobre `TaskRepository.findById` y `softDelete`, que ya existen), la forma que repiten las áreas de escritura;
> - el plugin pasa a recibir sus dependencias desde la raíz, con el manejador por clase (`BaseRouteHandler`) que usan 17 de los 25 archivos;
> - al terminar, el módulo entero queda sobre casos de uso: los otros seis handlers ya los resuelven y su única arista R2 (`TaskRepository`) desaparece;
> - `taskRoutes.ts` no tiene hoy ninguna suite que lo registre, así que la tajada escribe la primera suite de ruta con dependencias falsas pasadas al plugin: la plantilla del arnés;
> - no toca la fitness #40, no espera ninguna decisión y no mezcla un defecto en el mismo diff.
>
> Las áreas más chicas no fijan el patrón: operaciones (~40) no crea ningún caso de uso, reportes personalizados (150) sólo tiene R11 y SSO (230) reutiliza consultas existentes. Notificaciones (380) también lo fijaría, pero con la convención pasa de 400 y arrastra DEF-10. El `accountId` que `taskRoutes.ts` toma de la consulta o del cuerpo es la clase de N-AUTH-3 (el archivo figura en la línea base de su propuesta, `openspec/changes/principal-derived-authority/proposal.md`) y no cambia en esta tajada.
>
> **Hallazgos → §5.11** (DEF-7 a DEF-15) **y backlog** ([roadmap-detected-smells-backlog.md](../reports/roadmap-detected-smells-backlog.md), SMELL-194 a SMELL-202): nueve defectos leídos estáticamente durante el dimensionamiento y su verificación; las filas de abajo nombran los que viven en su código.
>
> **Fuentes:** el dimensionamiento `boundaries-repair-sizing.md` y la clasificación `boundaries-violations-509.classified.json` (`/root/.claude/omnipost-tools/research-2026-10-04/`, fuera del repositorio; engram 1213 y 1211), medidos el 2026-10-05 sobre `main` `4ffe981b`; el código de `apps/api/src`, `packages/` e `infra/` no cambió hasta `0e51032a`.

**Tajadas de rutas** (estimaciones en líneas CODE del dimensionamiento, con su intervalo; ids de tajada entre paréntesis; ⚠ = tajada en el límite de 400, que se parte si al medirla lo supera):

- [ ] **ARCH-1** **API+PKG** — cuentas, `accounts/accountRoutes.ts` (7 handlers): `CreateAccountUseCase` (normaliza el email dentro), `GetAccountQuery`, `ListAccountsQuery` (con un puerto de proyectos vivos: `findWithProjects` incluye los borrados) y `UpdateAccountUseCase` (con un mutador de `maxProjects`); R11 `AdminActorId` y `EmailAddress` pasan al caso de uso. ~1030 (850–1300), 3 tajadas (ACC-1 a ACC-3). N-AUTH-1 (§1, crítico) cambia la autorización de estas mismas rutas y BILL-21 (§5.9) retira `maxProjects`: el orden con ambos se fija al planificar. ADR-0033. **Nota 2026-10-18:** N-AUTH-1 cerró; `POST /accounts` y `GET /accounts` ya no existen y `PUT` escribe sólo `name`, así que el archivo tiene 5 manejadores y `CreateAccountUseCase`, `ListAccountsQuery`, el mutador de `maxProjects` y el `EmailAddress` de la ruta salen del alcance; la estimación de ~1030 líneas es anterior a ese cierre.
- [ ] **ARCH-2** **API+PKG** — proyectos, `projects/projectRoutes.ts` (7): `CreateProjectUseCase` (con un puerto que cuente los cupos ocupados, borrados incluidos), `ListProjectsQuery`, `GetProjectQuery` y `GetProjectPublishLogsQuery`; sale la verificación previa del borrado, que `DeleteProjectUseCase` ya hace; R11 `AdminActorId` en tres sitios. ~1010 (850–1300), 3 tajadas (PRJ-1 ⚠, PRJ-2, PRJ-3). El control de cuota que PRJ-1 lleva al caso de uso es el que BILL-21 (§5.9) retira: si BILL-21 va antes, PRJ-1 se achica. ADR-0033.
- [ ] **ARCH-3** **API+PKG** — canales, `channels/channelRoutes.ts` (8): siete casos de uso y consultas (crear, obtener, listar con su uso, actualizar, borrar, borrar en forma definitiva y lo que queda de marcar como principal, cuyo `SetPrimaryChannelUseCase` ya existe), una política de propiedad en el core y un puerto de verificación de credenciales; R1 y R7 en la conexión de Bluesky (`new BlueskyClient` en `:462`); R11 en la construcción de los ids. ~1760 (1400–2200), 5 tajadas (CH-1 a CH-5; CH-3 ⚠). CH-5 espera una decisión: la credencial de Bluesky (`{identifier, appPassword}`) la cifra hoy la propia ruta (`:483`), porque `ChannelRepository.save` sólo cifra la forma OAuth, y falta decidir su forma en el puerto. ADR-0033.
- [ ] **ARCH-4** **API+PKG** — analíticas del cliente, `analytics/analyticsRoutes.ts` (12): `CheckProjectAccessQuery` (la compuerta de acceso que repiten 10 handlers) y las consultas de tablero, exportación, analíticas por proyecto y alcance del stream; las compuertas de ROI y de analíticas cruzadas pasan a sus casos de uso, que ya existen. Cuatro endpoints responden 501 detrás de la compuerta (`:442,476,511,541`). ~1290 (1000–1600), 4 tajadas (AN-1 a AN-4). ADR-0033.
- [ ] **ARCH-5** **API+PKG** — analíticas de Admin, `admin/analyticsRoutes.ts` (5) y su código en `AnalyticsDashboardHandlers.ts`, `AnalyticsComplianceHandlers.ts` y `AnalyticsAccountHandlers.ts`: cuatro consultas (métricas de plataforma, métricas de cumplimiento, registro de auditoría paginado y panorama RGPD) y un caso de uso (ajustes de cuenta), con sus puertos y adaptadores; `AuditLogRepository` no tiene un listado global paginado. ~1950 (1550–2400), 6 tajadas (AA-1 ⚠ a AA-6). AA-5 mueve el código de DEF-8. ADR-0033.
- [ ] **ARCH-6** **API+PKG** — autenticación de Admin, `admin/adminUserRoutes.ts` (7) y `admin/auth/adminAuthRoutes.ts` (16): `RequestAdminPasswordResetUseCase` con un puerto de correo propio, como `WelcomeMailer` y `TeamInvitationMailer`; la normalización del email pasa al caso de uso (R11). ~455 (350–600), 2 tajadas (ADM-1, ADM-2). Mueve el código de DEF-13. ADR-0033.
- [ ] **ARCH-7** **API+PKG** — programación de Admin, `admin/schedulingRoutes.ts` (3) y su código en `SchedulingPostHandlers.ts` (403 líneas): el listado de publicaciones programadas y `CancelScheduledPostUseCase` y `ReschedulePostUseCase` con Unit of Work, sobre `PostAggregate` y un puerto de logs de publicación. ~1155 (900–1450), 3 tajadas (SA-1 ⚠, SA-2, SA-3). Saca dos sitios de la fitness #40 (ARCH-22). ADR-0033.
- [ ] **ARCH-8** **API+PKG** — programación del cliente, `scheduling/schedulingClientRoutes.ts` (5) y su código en `SchedulingSlotHandlers.ts` (659 líneas): el puerto y el adaptador de `SchedulingRule`, que no existe en `packages/`; los dos calculadores puros pasan al core con sus pruebas; las consultas de franjas y de horarios óptimos; el alta de una franja y el alta masiva. ~1800–1950 (1450–2400), 7 tajadas (SC-1 a SC-7). Saca un sitio de la fitness #40 (ARCH-22). ADR-0033.
- [ ] **ARCH-9** **API+PKG** — anuncios, `announcements/announcementRoutes.ts` (5, consultas en línea): puerto, adaptador y cinco operaciones; una entidad sumaría unas 100 líneas. ~750 (650–1000), 2 tajadas (ANN-1, ANN-2 ⚠). ADR-0033.
- [ ] **ARCH-10** **API+PKG** — onboarding, `onboarding/onboardingRoutes.ts` (3): puerto, adaptador, la consulta de progreso, y completar o descartar un paso con Unit of Work. ~600 (500–800), 2 tajadas (ONB-1, ONB-2). Mueve el código de DEF-11: una consulta no escribe. ADR-0033.
- [ ] **ARCH-11** **API+PKG** — outbox, `outbox/outboxAdminRoutes.ts` (3): el puerto y el adaptador de los mensajes fallidos (DLQ), el listado, y resolver y reintentar con Unit of Work (R3, ver ARCH-22). `OutboxWriter` no sirve: sólo escribe eventos de dominio y no reinserta un payload crudo. ~715 (550–900), 2 tajadas (OBX-1, OBX-2 ⚠).
